# Schedule-derived curved inward blocks: one joint UNVERIFIED derivation

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; allocated invocation72.
TASK.md: ended baseline71, ceiling72, attempted ceiling73; cap1.
Plan d1529a5e2f8fd47f. This entire document is ONE research proof attempt.
There was no verifier call, no fact_submit, and no new fact_id. Every new
claim below is UNVERIFIED. The original exact-profile problem is OPEN.

The new mechanism is a scalar spline whose derivative changes within
every positive ordinary block. Each block has its own schedule-derived
slope. Paid connectors join successive inward visits to the same ray.
It changes the physical queried gradients, rather than merely completing
old quadratic or single-cut prefixes at their terminal point.

The result of this search is a precisely scoped obstruction. On the ENTIRE
SAME-enclosing-m H_(m,1/4), for EVERY m>=1536, the constructed payoff V
satisfies

    0<V/H_base<2(25/36)^(m-3)<1,
    B_(2^m-1)V<(237/125)(25/36)^(m-3),                 (0.1)

where H_base=1/(1+2 sum H_m). The stronger statement in Section8 allows
EVERY scalar convex one-smooth derivative within the specified disjoint
inward block geometry, with no curvature palette, no bound on its knot
count, and no constant-gradient assumption. It has the same upper bound.
This is not an upper bound on arbitrary scalar GD or unrestricted W_n.

## 1. Normalization, exact target and recalled scopes

For a nonnegative integer n, a finite word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1))>=0,
and L>0, the physical updates are x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)f'(x_t)
in the scalar constructions. In the original problem W_n(h) is the
supremum over all finite-dimensional smooth convex instances of

    2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2),               (1.1)

where x_star is a genuine minimizer and x_0 differs from x_star.
Thus W=2R. Our constructed minimum is uniquely0, so our chosen radius
is also the closest radius. No radius is reset at a checkpoint.

Retain exactly the original ALL-integer-split benchmark:

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.    (1.2)

The all-word lower-profile objective would require, for every epsilon>0,
one N_epsilon independent of h such that every n>=N_epsilon and every
h>=0 has a legal lower witness of value at least(1-epsilon)/B_n.
Nothing proved here supplies that remaining quantifier or constant.

Accepted inputs, only at their actual scopes:

* fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the normalized interpolation convention.
  All rows for our scalar spline are also proved directly in Section5.
* factb98bb807d514e80d supplies K_n, its legal attaining single-cut and
  canonical Huber functions, and the benchmark definitions(1.2).
* fact5fd396eba75651e7 supplies the exact same-enclosing-m hierarchy,
  the mass identities and parameter bounds in its Section6, and its
  unconditional benchmark inequality(77), reproduced in Section6 here.
  None of its spiral dynamics or family upper bounds is imported.
* fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a is used only for K_n<=F_ray_n<=W_n, with the
  full ray family as defined in that fact. No new upper bound for that
  full family is inferred from the present spline obstruction.

The following are recalled drafts, not correctness premises:

1. FULL5100e05d4c62d3ff concerns synchronized maximal completions of
   complete-word canonical/single-cut prefixes. It does not quantify
   arbitrary nonlinear scalar queried trajectories.
2. 25e480a41fc81415 and its extracted law802187ebdff8da78 use only
   fixed curvatures1 and1/2. They do not cover this new spline law.
3. The all-value/all-curvature scalar draft43a616da7da451c7 fixes
   x_i=p_i(theta)=product_(t<i)(1-theta*h_t), g_i=theta*p_i(theta).
   It optimizes compatible VALUES and primitive energy over those fixed
   proportional-gradient histories and theta in[0,1]. Its shortest-path
   formula does not free these positions or gradients. It is not an
   optimization over every scalar derivative, and its primitive energy
   is not the normalized last objective gap. Audit8d78e0975df8400a also
   records a mandatory correction to a printed low-curvature increment.
   No such draft formula is a premise below.
4. 312a773572f0099c treats disjoint inward CONSTANT-gradient blocks at
   the first-occurrence checkpoints of this same small-tau hierarchy.
   The present mechanism removes that constant-gradient assumption.
   Section8 is derived from physical displacements and spatial ordering,
   not by citing that earlier unverified obstruction as a lemma.

There is no assertion that these recalled limitations exhaust scalar
nonlinearity. In particular, overlapping returns and extra crossings
inside an ordinary block are deliberately outside Section8.

## 2. An explicit all-word selector and backward knot rule

Everything in this section is a finite symbolic definition from the
entire known word. No mathematical recurrence was executed in this round.

Select, in chronological order, precisely the strict record steps

    h_j>2 and h_j>max({0} union {h_t:t<j}).              (2.1)

Let their count be k. If k=0, use the canonical Huber fallback specified
below. If k>=1, write the ORIGINAL word as

    (block_0,b_0,block_1,...,b_(k-1),block_k),
    block_i=(alpha_(i,0),...,alpha_(i,r_i-1)),
    s_i=sum_(v<r_i)alpha_(i,v).                         (2.2)

The integer r_i is the physical length of block_i. Empty blocks are
allowed. No step is reordered or deleted; zero steps retain their indices.

For every0<=i<=k put

    eta_i=1/[4(1+s_i)],
    P_(i,l)=product_(0<=v<l)(1-eta_i*alpha_(i,v)),
    P_i=P_(i,r_i),
    T_i=(P_i^(-1)-1)/eta_i.                            (2.3)

An empty product is1. Every factor is positive, because each
alpha_(i,v)<=s_i and eta_i*s_i<1/4. The elementary product inequality
product(1-z_v)>=1-sum z_v for0<=z_v<=1 follows by multiplying the
two-factor identity(1-z)(1-w)=1-z-w+zw and inducting. Thus

    3/4<P_i<=1, 0<eta_i<=1/4.                          (2.4)

Another exact identity is

    T_i=sum_(l<r_i)alpha_(i,l)*P_(i,l)/P_i >= s_i.     (2.5)

Indeed P_(i,l)-P_(i,l+1)=eta_i alpha_(i,l)P_(i,l);
sum and divide by eta_i P_i. Each P_(i,l)>=P_i. These are
symbolic identities, not evaluated trajectory or numerical products.

Define the final gradient magnitude q_k, terminal radius R_k, initial
radius A_k and initial gradient magnitude p_k for the last block by

    q_k=1, R_k=1/eta_k,
    A_k=1/(eta_k P_k), p_k=1/P_k.                      (2.6)

Let D_i=b_i-1>1 for i<k. For stage k-1 set

    q_(k-1)=A_k/D_(k-1), R_(k-1)=q_(k-1),
    A_(k-1)=R_(k-1)+T_(k-1)q_(k-1),
    p_(k-1)=q_(k-1)/P_(k-1).                         (2.7)

For i=k-2,...,0 define backward

    q_i=[A_(i+1)+A_(i+2)-p_(i+2)]/D_i,
    R_i=A_(i+2)+q_i-p_(i+2),
    A_i=R_i+T_i q_i, p_i=q_i/P_i.                     (2.8)

These are finite explicit sums, products and quotients with positive
known denominators. They are not an interpolation-feasibility oracle.
The law uses this spline exactly when its explicit guards hold:

    q_i>0 for all i, and q_i>=p_(i+2) for0<=i<=k-2.    (2.9)

Otherwise use the canonical Huber fallback. The guard is a finite list
of explicit positivity and nesting comparisons, with no parameter
optimization or search tree.

On the accepted branch, A_i>=p_i>0 and R_i>=q_i>0. For the final
stage these follow from eta_k<=1. For stage k-1 use
p_i=q_i+eta_i T_i q_i and A_i=q_i+T_i q_i. Backward, if
A_(i+2)>=p_(i+2), equation(2.8) gives R_i>=q_i, and the
same identity proves A_i>=p_i. Moreover the guard gives

    R_i>=A_(i+2) for0<=i<=k-2.                        (2.10)

For every i<k, the SAME definitions give the physical crossing identity

    b_i q_i=R_i+A_(i+1).                              (2.11)

For i=k-1 this is D_i q_i=A_k. For earlier i it is the
first two equations of(2.8) rearranged. For all i including k,
A_i=R_i+T_i q_i, because(2.6) and(2.3) imply it at k.

The fallback is fully explicit: S=sum h, u=1/(1+2S)>0,
g_H(x)=max(-u,min(x,u)), F_H(x)=integral_0^x g_H(t)dt,
with initial point1. All physical positions are

    x_t=1-u sum_(v<t)h_v>=1-uS=(1+S)/(1+2S)>=u.

Thus every queried gradient is u, the actual terminal is1-uS,
the minimum is uniquely0, and2F_H(x_n)=1/(1+2S).
This also handles the empty or all-zero word, with its true terminal.

## 3. One static derivative on the whole real line, and ALL its areas

Assume the guards(2.9). Stage i uses ray sign sigma_i=(-1)^i.
For each parity construct its radial derivative Q_sigma on r>=0
starting with its innermost stage, then moving OUTWARD by decreasing i
in increments of2. The symbol sigma here denotes a sign, not an index.

On the ray containing stage k define

    Q_(sigma_k)(r)=eta_k*r for0<=r<=A_k.               (3.1)

On the other ray, whose innermost stage is k-1, define

    Q_(sigma_(k-1))(r)=r for0<=r<=q_(k-1),
    Q_(sigma_(k-1))(r)=q_(k-1)+eta_(k-1)(r-q_(k-1))
                         for q_(k-1)<=r<=A_(k-1).     (3.2)

For each outer stage i<=k-2 on its own ray, append

    Q_(sigma_i)(r)=p_(i+2)+(r-A_(i+2))
                                 for A_(i+2)<=r<=R_i,
    Q_(sigma_i)(r)=q_i+eta_i(r-R_i)
                                 for R_i<=r<=A_i.     (3.3)

At r=R_i the first expression is q_i by(2.8). At r=A_i the
second is q_i+eta_i T_i q_i=q_i/P_i=p_i. The innermost formulas
likewise have their required gradient at A_i. Guard(2.10) orders all
these intervals. A connector of zero width is kept as a common endpoint.
If s_i=0, then P_i=1,T_i=0,A_i=R_i,p_i=q_i, so that block is a
single point with the same gradient. There is no inconsistent knot.

Beyond the outermost A_i on each ray, continue constantly with value
p_i. Finally define globally

    g(x)=Q_+(x) for x>=0, g(x)=-Q_-(-x) for x<=0,
    F(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt.                         (3.4)

Every piece of Q_sigma has slope0, eta_i, or1. All pieces match.
Consequently g is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz on each
ray. Across zero, Q_sigma(r)<=r, so for x<=0<=y,
0<=g(y)-g(x)<=y-x. This proves the same global properties. Thus F
is globally convex, C1, and1-smooth, without any unexamined finite-jet
extension. In fact its Lipschitz constant is exactly1, because(3.2)
has a nonempty unit-slope core. On both rays Q_sigma(r)>0 for r>0.
Hence the ENTIRE minimizer set is{0} and min F=F(0)=0.

Here is the explicit area rule, which fixes every queried value as well
as every unsampled value. Write I_i(r)=F(sigma_i*r) on its own ray.
For the final core use

    I_k(r)=eta_k r^2/2, 0<=r<=A_k.                    (3.5)

For the other innermost core use I_(k-1)(r)=r^2/2 up to q_(k-1).
On any nonterminal slow interval [R_i,A_i] put

    I_i(r)=I_i(R_i)+q_i(r-R_i)+eta_i(r-R_i)^2/2.       (3.6)

For i=k-1, I_i(R_i)=q_i^2/2. For an outer i<=k-2,

    I_i(R_i)=I_(i+2)(A_(i+2))+(q_i^2-p_(i+2)^2)/2,
    I_i(r)=I_(i+2)(A_(i+2))+p_(i+2)(r-A_(i+2))
                                  +(r-A_(i+2))^2/2
                       on[A_(i+2),R_i].              (3.7)

The equality for I_i(R_i) is the integral of the unit connector,
whose length is q_i-p_(i+2). The final interval also obeys(3.6)
with I_k(R_k)=1/(2eta_k), since q_k=eta_k R_k=1.
Beyond an outermost A_i use its matching linear continuation with
slope p_i. These formulas cover the whole real line and every area
between its knots. No old height is erased, independently reassigned,
or replaced by a locally chosen objective zero.

## 4. Actual physical GD, total spatial ordering and original terminal

Start at x_0=A_0. At the l-th physical query in block_i, including
its endpoint l=r_i before the checkpoint or terminal, the claimed
and ACTUAL point and gradient are

    x_(i,l)=sigma_i[R_i+(q_i/eta_i)(P_(i,l)/P_i-1)],
    g_(i,l)=sigma_i q_i P_(i,l)/P_i.                   (4.1)

Every prefix product lies between P_i and1. Thus its bracket lies
in[R_i,A_i], where the derivative is exactly(3.1)--(3.3).
Applying its next original physical step alpha_(i,l) gives

    x_(i,l)-alpha_(i,l)g_(i,l)
      =sigma_i[R_i+(q_i/eta_i)(P_(i,l+1)/P_i-1)],

by the defining factor of the product. This proves every update;
no block was replaced by its sum for the dynamics. For l=0 the
point is sigma_i A_i; for l=r_i it is sigma_i R_i and its
gradient is sigma_i q_i. The checkpoint uses(2.11) and lands at

    sigma_i R_i-b_i sigma_i q_i=-sigma_i A_(i+1).      (4.2)

Equations(4.1)--(4.2) therefore describe the complete original run.
All knots were fixed before this run, as functions of the known h.

Spatial order is fully specified. On each ray, the radial intervals
are ordered inward by i,i+2,i+4,... according to(2.10). Within an
interval, radii decrease with chronological l, strictly at every
positive alpha and equally across a zero alpha. Opposite rays are
separated by zero. On the negative ray, increasing radius means a
smaller real position, so real-coordinate ordering reverses that
radial list. This explicitly distinguishes chronology from spatial
order. The only possible repeated points in this law are repeated
positions from zero steps, collapsed zero-mass blocks, and shared
same-ray endpoints when a connector guard is equality. In each case
(3.4)--(3.7) assign identical gradients and values. Checkpoints cross
zero without landing at zero because every A_i>0. All intermediate
knots crossed by a jump are already part of the same function.

The physical terminal, after EVERY original step, is exactly

    x_n=sigma_k/eta_k,
    g(x_n)=sigma_k,
    F(x_n)=1/(2eta_k),
    V_spline(h)=1/(eta_k A_0^2).                       (4.3)

If the last ordinary step is zero, its two products in(4.1) coincide,
so(4.3) is the repeated last jet with its original value. If the last
step is a selected record, block_k is empty and(4.2) itself lands at
the terminal(4.3), since P_k=1. A short positive final step is simply
the last factor in P_k. None of these cases is completed at a different
horizon or has its last leaf removed.

The actual initial closest radius is A_0. If unit normalization is
desired, replace F by F_norm(x)=F(A_0 x)/A_0^2, divide every knot
position and derivative value by A_0, and start from1. The area
values divide by A_0^2. Its minimum is uniquely0, its exact global
Lipschitz constant remains1, and the payoff stays(4.3). For general
advertised L use f=L*F_norm and physical steps h_t/L. This preserves
every normalized point and proves the claimed W=2R scaling.

For every word set V_law equal to(4.3) on the accepted branch and
to H_base on the fallback branch. Explicitly, for S=sum h,

    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2S),
       max_(0<=j<n) (h_j-1)_+^2/
          [(1+sum_(t<j)h_t)^2(1+2sum_(t>j)h_t)]},

with the inner maximum omitted at n=0 and(t)_+=max(t,0).
The finite explicit lower selector

    V_new(h)=max{K_n(h),V_law(h)}<=W_n(h)              (4.4)

chooses the attaining accepted K_n instance if that entry is larger.
This defines a lower witness for all finite nonnegative words, including
zero, huge, unordered and repeated steps. It asserts no all-word
near-benchmark comparison for(4.4).

## 5. Every original gradient and value is globally compatible

For any u<v put Delta=g(v)-g(u)>=0. Global monotonicity and
one-Lipschitz continuity give0<=Delta<=v-u and, for u<=t<=v,

    g(t)-g(u)>=max(0,Delta-(v-t)),
    g(v)-g(t)>=max(0,Delta-(t-u)).

Integrating each lower bound gives respectively

    F(v)-F(u)-g(u)(v-u)>=Delta^2/2,
    F(u)-F(v)-g(v)(u-v)>=Delta^2/2.                   (5.1)

Thus, in the orientation of accepted1252b8823bb5a290, EVERY ordered
query/terminal/optimum pair satisfies

    Q_ij=F(x_i)-F(x_j)+g(x_i)(x_j-x_i)
                              +(g(x_i)-g(x_j))^2/2<=0. (5.2)

Equal points give equality. This proves remote rows and both directions,
not only neighboring chronological interpolation. The full real Gram of
(x_0,g_0,...,g_n) is zz^T for the actual scalar list z, hence is PSD.
After unit normalization z is divided by A_0. All zero-step coordinates
remain present. This is an actual primal construction and not a PSD-only
claim about a free Gram.

## 6. The decisive same-enclosing-m hierarchy

Fix ANY integer m>=1536, with exactly

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/(4m),
    c=lambda(1+1/(4m)), a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),0<=j<m,
    n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.                  (6.1)

All children use this SAME enclosing m and tau=1/4. Accepted fact
5fd396eba75651e7, equations(36)--(38),(77), gives

    2<c<121/50<5/2, 0<a<21/50<1/2,
    d_j/M_j<21/50,
    M_j=v*c^j+(1-v)2^j, v=a/(c-2) in(0,1),
    B_n/(1+2S_m)<237/250.                            (6.2)

We use only these unconditional statements about the actual word and
original benchmark. No benchmark recurrence is run.

The level0 step is1+a<2; the level1 step is1+beta<2;
d_2=beta*c>2, since beta>5/6 and c>12/5. Steps increase
strictly with their tree level. Hence the record selector(2.1) picks
EXACTLY the first occurrences of levels2,3,...,m-1:

    k=m-2, b_i=1+d_(i+2), D_i=d_(i+2),
    block_0=H_2, block_i=H_(i+1) for1<=i<=k,
    s_0=S_2, s_i=S_(i+1) for1<=i<=k.                 (6.3)

The selected physical zero-based checkpoint index is2^(i+2)-1.
This is the full original expansion of H_m, not a contracted word.
In particular the last block is the entire H_(m-1), including its
original final level0 step1+a. Its length is2^(m-1)-1.

Several elementary consequences will be used explicitly:

    D_(i+1)=c D_i, s_(i+1)=S_(i+2)>D_i,
    s=S_(m-1)>D=d_(m-1),
    T=1+2s, S_m=2s+1+D, 1+2S_m<3T,
    1+S_2=4+2a+beta>4.                               (6.4)

For S_j>d_j, note S_1=1+a>beta=d_1 and
S_(j+1)-d_(j+1)=2(S_j-d_j)+1+(3-c)d_j>0.
The ratio bound in(6.2) also gives M_j>(50/21)d_j.

## 7. The spline guards hold, and the queried law is genuinely nonlinear

On(6.3), the final base gives

    q_(k-1)=A_k/D=4(1+s)/(D P_k)>4q_k,
    p_k=q_k/P_k<(4/3)q_k.                             (7.1)

Suppose backward that q_(i+1)>(4/3)q_(i+2). Then
p_(i+2)<(4/3)q_(i+2)<q_(i+1). The crossing identity at i+1
and A_(i+1)=R_(i+1)+T_(i+1)q_(i+1) yield

    D_i q_i=(b_(i+1)+T_(i+1))q_(i+1)-p_(i+2)
            >(D_(i+1)+T_(i+1))q_(i+1)
            >(c+1)D_i q_(i+1).                       (7.2)

The last inequality uses(2.5),(6.4). Thus
q_i>(c+1)q_(i+1)>(4/3)q_(i+1), and q_i>p_(i+2).
Starting with(7.1), backward induction proves EVERY guard strictly.
The spline branch, not the fallback, is used for every m>=1536.

Every block in(6.3) has positive length and positive steps. Hence
P_i<1 and p_i>q_i, so EVERY ordinary block samples decreasing,
nonconstant derivative magnitudes. There are m-2 distinct eta_i:
s_0=s_1=S_2, followed by the strictly increasing masses S_3,...,
S_(m-1). Their number is unbounded with m. The connectors have
positive lengths, and the number of actual spatial knots grows with m.

This is not a proportional-gradient polynomial history. Already in
block k-1, on its ray

    Q(r)/r=eta_(k-1)+(1-eta_(k-1))q_(k-1)/r

is strictly decreasing with r in a nonempty sampled interval. Two
distinct queries therefore have distinct nonzero gradient-to-position
ratios about the genuine minimum0. Changing objective values cannot
turn these gradients into g_i=theta*x_i for a single theta.
Even allowing a different affine reference point does not repair this:
successive distinct queries in block i have gradient-difference divided
by position-difference exactly eta_i. Blocks k-1 and k have different
eta_i and both contain positive steps, so no single affine relation
g=theta*x+d fits all their sampled gradients.

All query positions are strictly ordered inside each ordinary block,
and each same-ray block is strictly inside its preceding same-ray
block. Opposite-ray overshoots are the actual selected crossings(4.2).
Thus(4.1) gives the complete spatial order, including every unselected
large step in each H_j. This construction has no ties on this positive
hierarchy; ties and zero steps for general words were addressed above.

In particular, after the ORIGINAL final1+a step, its exact terminal is

    x_n=(-1)^(m-2)*4(1+S_(m-1)),
    g_n=(-1)^(m-2),
    F_n=2(1+S_(m-1)),
    V_spline(H_m)=4(1+S_(m-1))/A_0^2.                (7.3)

Here A_0 is precisely the finite expression(2.3),(2.6)--(2.8), using
the original individual steps in all the products. There is no claimed
substitution of a block mass for those products.
More explicitly, with ell=1+a and e=eta_k, the last preterminal query is

    x_(n-1)=sigma_k/[e(1-e*ell)],
    g_(n-1)=sigma_k/(1-e*ell),
    F_(n-1)=1/[2e(1-e*ell)^2].

Its original final update gives x_(n-1)-ell*g_(n-1)=sigma_k/e,
exactly(7.3). The denominator is positive by(2.4); the final leaf and
its queried value have both been retained.

## 8. Whole-class bound: arbitrary curvature inside disjoint inward blocks

This section proves a broader scoped obstruction jointly with the law.
No previously unverified shelf lemma is invoked.

Fix the word(6.1) and the exact decomposition(6.3). Let F be ANY scalar
convex C1 one-smooth function with an attained minimum. Translate and
reflect so that a closest minimizer to its positive initial point is0,
the minimum value is0, and x_0=A_0>0. A minimum interval extending
leftwards from0 is allowed. Suppose its ACTUAL trajectory has these
properties, for these precise original blocks and checkpoints:

(a) Block i starts at sigma_i A_i and ends at sigma_i R_i,
    with A_i>=R_i>0. Every query and update endpoint of that block
    stays on this ray, with a positive derivative magnitude.
(b) The original checkpoint b_i crosses to sigma_(i+1) A_(i+1).
(c) R_i>=A_(i+2) for0<=i<=k-2: a later visit to a ray is wholly
    inside the preceding visit's ending radius.

No derivative is prescribed on any block, connector, or unsampled
interval. No bounded curvature palette or finite knot bound is imposed.
Let q_i=|F'(sigma_i R_i)| and let delta_i=A_i-R_i be the actual
ordinary-block displacement. Static monotonicity on each ray gives

    delta_i=sum_(l<r_i)alpha_(i,l)|F'(x_(i,l))|
                   >=s_i q_i,
    R_i>=q_i, b_i q_i=R_i+A_(i+1).                    (8.1)

The second inequality uses F'(0)=0 and the global Lipschitz bound.
The final equality is the actual checkpoint update. Crucially, this
does NOT identify displacement with s_i q_i when gradients vary.

For0<=i<=k-2, the actual spatial guard(c) and(8.1) yield

    b_i q_i=R_i+A_(i+1)
       >=A_(i+2)+A_(i+1)
       =b_(i+1)q_(i+1)+delta_(i+1)
       >=(b_(i+1)+s_(i+1))q_(i+1).                  (8.2)

This is the added return-distance cost for this entire broader class.
It does not depend on the number of spatial knots or any affine
formula inside the blocks.

Put d=D_i=d_(i+2)>2 and M=M_(i+2). Then from(6.2)--(6.3),

    (b_(i+1)+s_(i+1))/b_i=(c d+M)/(1+d)
       >(c+50/21)*d/(1+d)
       >(2/3)(c+50/21)>c+1/2.                       (8.3)

For the last comparison, its difference is
100/63-c/3-1/2>100/63-5/6-1/2=16/63>0.
Consequently q_i>(c+1/2)q_(i+1) throughout this range.
At the final checkpoint, R_(k-1)>=q_(k-1) gives
D q_(k-1)>=A_k. Also A_0>= (1+s_0)q_0. Combining gives

    A_0>(1+S_2)(c+1/2)^(m-3)*A_k/D.                 (8.4)

We now bound the FULL actual final objective, retaining arbitrary
inner areas. Let r=R_k be its actual final radius, q=q_k its final
gradient magnitude, and s=S_(m-1). Monotonicity and Lipschitz
continuity from0 give Q(t)<=min(t,q) for0<=t<=r, with r>=q.
Equation(8.1) gives r<=A_k-sq. Hence

    2F(x_n)<=2qr-q^2
             <=2q A_k-(2s+1)q^2
             <=A_k^2/(1+2s).                         (8.5)

The last inequality is equivalent to
((1+2s)q-A_k)^2>=0. If a terminal derivative is zero, its terminal
point is a minimizer and the payoff is0; the same bound follows without
dividing by q. The hypotheses with positive earlier stages are enough.
A flat part of the minimizer interval contributes no integral and cannot
increase(8.5). All retained actual objective areas are included in this
integral upper bound.

Divide(8.5) by the TRUE initial squared distance A_0^2 and use(8.4):

    V=2F(x_n)/A_0^2
       <D^2/[(1+S_2)^2(c+1/2)^(2(m-3))(1+2s)].       (8.6)

By(6.4), D=a*c^(m-1), a<1/2, c<5/2 and1+S_2>4,

    V/H_base
      <[3a^2 c^4/(1+S_2)^2]
                              [c/(c+1/2)]^(2(m-3))
      <2(25/36)^(m-3).                               (8.7)

Indeed the first factor is below1875/1024<2, and
c/(c+1/2)<5/6. Since25/36<3/4 and m-3>=5, the last expression
is below2(3/4)^5=243/512<1. This proves the class bound for EVERY
m>=1536, with no pointwise-parameter/limit exchange.

Our constructed spline satisfies(a)--(c), by Sections3--7, and has
the exact payoff(7.3). Combining(8.7) with the accepted exact benchmark
comparison(6.2) proves(0.1). Thus its B-scaled value tends to0.
This conclusion concerns its actual original terminal, true initial
radius, and full function values.

## 9. Comparison with the canonical K and ray channels

Because F_ray_n>=K_n>=H_base by the accepted constructions,
equation(8.7) proves directly that this ENTIRE disjoint inward class
cannot beat either complete canonical reference on the same word.
This conclusion needs no upper estimate on the full ray channel.

For completeness, K_n(H_m)=H_base can be derived here for every
m>=1536. Consider ANY physical cut at tree level j with excess d_j.
Its actual preceding/following masses P,T satisfy
p=1+P>=M_j and t=1+T>=M_j, because its immediate neighboring
children are both the original H_j. The ratio of its accepted cut
payoff to H_base is

    d_j^2(2p+2t+2d_j-1)/[p^2(2t-1)].                 (9.1)

For fixed d_j,t, this decreases in p, since it is a positive
combination of p^(-1) and p^(-2). For fixed p it decreases in t,
since it equals(d_j/p)^2[1+2(p+d_j)/(2t-1)]. Put u=d_j/M_j.
At p=t=M_j, (9.1) is

    u^2[(4+2u)M_j-1]/(2M_j-1)
      =u^2[2+u+(1+u)/(2M_j-1)]
      <=u^2(3+2u)<(1/2)^2(3+1)=1.                  (9.2)

Here M_j>=1 and0<u<21/50<1/2 from(6.2). This includes
j=0, every tied-level occurrence, and the actual last step, whose
following child is H_0 and whose true following mass is0. Hence every
single-cut value is strictly below H_base, and the canonical term
attains H_base. Thus(4.4) satisfies

    V_new(H_m)=K_n(H_m)=H_base,
    B_n V_new(H_m)<237/250.                            (9.3)

If the entire accepted F_ray_n channel is added to the selector, the
spline still contributes nothing on this hierarchy: the augmented
selector equals F_ray_n there. We do NOT assert a new formula or a new
B-scaled upper bound for that larger augmented selector. The precise
statement proved is that the new nonlinear law cannot improve it.

## 10. Adversarial scope and endpoint tests

First direct attempt: permit curvature to vary inside every previously
constant block. Equations(2.3)--(7.3) implement that attempt as an actual
function, with changing gradients and retained integral values. The
exact payoff and(8.7) show why this law still fails the diagnostic.

Second direct attempt within the SAME mechanism: could an entirely
different derivative, arbitrary knots and arbitrary inner areas repair
the loss while retaining the same inward block order? Equations(8.1)--
(8.7) answer negatively for that precisely stated class. Affineness and
the selected eta_i disappear from its proof. This is not a second
unrelated law and does not consume an unallocated additional round.

The construct-counterexamples screen tests two tempting extrapolations.

UNGUARDED RECORD POLICY IS FALSE. For h=(100,101), both steps are
records and all three ordinary blocks are empty. Then eta_i=1/4,
P_i=1,T_i=0, q_2=1,A_2=4, q_1=A_1=p_1=1/25 and

    q_0=(1/25+4-1)/99=76/2475<p_2=1.

Thus R_0=A_2+q_0-p_2<A_2, and the proposed OUTWARD connector
would have negative length. Guard(2.9) correctly rejects it. An
unconditional assertion that the record recipe always yields the stated
ordered spline would be false. The defined fallback gives a legal
all-word construction; no interpolation feasibility oracle fills this
gap, and no all-word guarantee is inferred from fallback existence.

DISJOINT RETURNS ARE NOT AUTOMATIC FOR SCALAR GD. On the single static
function F(x)=x^2/4, starting at1, use the original word
(1,6,1,6,1). Its exact points are

    1, 1/2, -1, -1/2, 1, 1/2.

Its derivative is x/2, its unique minimizer is0, and each update
follows by multiplying by1-h/2. Select the two6 checkpoints, with
ordinary blocks(1),(1),(1). Stage0 and stage2 visit the SAME positive
interval[1/2,1], so R_0=1/2<A_2=1. All the physical data are
legal, but condition(c) fails. This exact example refutes deleting the
disjointness hypothesis from the geometry argument. It is a boundary
test, not the new witness law and not a claimed same-H_m separator.

The task's same-H_m requirement is met for the constructed law and
the entire class in Section8. It is not met for every scalar function:
neither extra crossings inside those H_j blocks, overlapping visits,
nor another checkpoint architecture is covered. The missing all-word
selection/gluing statement remains a theorem-level problem. Failure
of this prescribed family says nothing adverse about the unrestricted
W_n(H_m), and it cannot serve as a negative minimax resolution.

# Bounded full primitive discovery and four exact original witnesses

Status: COMPLETE JOINT RESEARCH DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max3 round41,
the first of the two rounds allocated after completed40, with completed
ceiling42 and attempted ceiling43. No fact_submit is authorized or called.
The original exact-profile problem, with W=2R, remains OPEN.

Accepted inputs are fact04219554e41d9750, Section10, for the full primitive
identity; fact1252b8823bb5a290 for the original interpolation orientation;
and fact4c92d3fc4eb6153e for the conditional fixed-b3 capacity thresholds.
Every new mathematical assertion used in this document is proved jointly.
The earlier C80 and max2 longer-seed drafts suggested witnesses but are
not premises. Numerical infeasibility is not an exact proof.

## 1. Finite experiment, scope and resources

The PUBLIC directory containing this document retains the stdlib atomic
meter, eight pre-registered exact word plans, every Wolfram request,
complete raw result, parsed copy and final-accounting receipt. The original
model uses all r(r+1) ordered off-diagonal Lambda entries, with no rank,
support, reflection, scalar-factor or zero-remainder restriction. Every
original gradient remains formal and distinct. This is primitive
certificate feasibility, not full-objective PEP.

Both charged calibrations passed: h=(1) has Lambda01=2,Lambda10=4 and
the nonzero PSD matrix [[1,-1],[-1,1]]; h=(2) is exactly rejected by
the diagonal contradiction in Section2. The backend was existing Wolfram
Language15.0.1/CSDP, tolerance10^-8. No package was installed.

The frozen discovery words and actual outcomes were:

| Name | Physical word h | Capacity A | Registered screens | Conic outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| f5a | (3/2,12/5,6/5,12/5,7/5) | 99/10 | all pass | numerical infeasible |
| f5b | (31/20,47/20,5/4,12/5,27/20) | 99/10 | all pass | numerical infeasible |
| f5c | (3/2,5/2,23/20,12/5,27/20) | 99/10 | global cut fails | no solve |
| f5d | (31/20,5/2,11/10,12/5,27/20) | 99/10 | all pass | numerical infeasible |
| f6a | (3/2,5/2,5/4,39/20,5/2,131/100) | 1201/100 | all pass | numerical infeasible |
| f6b | (159/100,5/2,13/10,191/100,5/2,121/100) | 1201/100 | all pass | numerical infeasible |
| f6c | (3/2,49/20,5/4,103/50,5/2,5/4) | 1201/100 | adjacent pair fails | no solve |
| f6d | (31/20,49/20,5/4,201/100,5/2,5/4) | 1201/100 | all pass | numerical infeasible |

All six conic rejections have the raw warnings pinf/nsolc, Infinity
objective and Indeterminate multipliers. No numerical array or eigenvalue
was silently converted into a certificate or impossibility theorem.
Sections4--7 independently give exact actual-function rejections of f5c,
f6c,f5a and f6a. The other four rows remain numerical rejection reports only.

There were10 charged cases:2 calibrations and8 distinct discovery words
in2 families. Eight cases used one conic solve each; two used none.
Measured kernel construction/solve/readback time was1.972763 seconds.
A conservative0.5-second export/local-postprocessing guard per case,
which is not a claimed actual duration, makes6.972763 accounted seconds.
Discovery stopped at case10. With no credible reconstruction target,
the remaining2 slots and83.027237 seconds were retired and the ledger
was closed. No old ledger changed, and no correction retry occurred.

All exact witness work below is textual proof construction, with no
additional evaluator, numerical check, machine algebra or coefficient
reconstruction job. No additional physical proposal is assessed here.

## 2. Exact equivalence of the implemented original PSD model

For a word h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1))>=0 set s=sum h_i and A=1+s. Let e be
the all-ones column of length r+1, T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, with
the other entries zero. Set

 L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
 omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
 D=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<r,-s(s+2)),
 M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2.

The original constraints are Lambda_ii=0, Lambda_ij>=0 for every i!=j,
L^T e=omega, and M>=0 on all of R^(r+1).

Under the original flow, e^T D e=-sum h_i^2-s^2, while

 e^T LT e=omega^T T e
   =-2sum_i h_i(1+sum_(t<i)h_t)+2s(1+s)
   =sum_i h_i^2+s^2.

Thus e^T M e=0. If M is PSD then M e=0: for every real vector v,
nonnegativity of (e+t v)^T M(e+t v) for both signs of sufficiently
small real t forces v^T M e=0. Conversely, when M e=0, use the
invertible matrix P=[e_0,...,e_(r-1),e], where e_i are coordinate
columns. The exact congruence is P^T M P=diag(M[0:r,0:r],0).
Consequently the implemented equations M e=0 and the leading r-square
PSD constraint are equivalent to the FULL original PSD constraint.
They impose no extra rank or support restriction. The original complete
M, every sign and every flow row are retained in the raw readback.

For h=(1), the array [[0,2],[4,0]] gives flow(-2,2) and
M=[[1,-1],[-1,1]], with quadratic(u-v)^2. For h=(2), writing
Lambda01=a>=0, flow gives Lambda10=a+4 and M=[[-a,2],[2,a-4]].
PSD would force a<=0 and a>=4. This contradiction is independent of
the solver and completes the two calibration references.

## 3. Full original identity and the actual-function witness method

For physical GD x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i with g_i=grad F(x_i), write
f_i=F(x_i) and use the accepted orientation

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

The primitive energy is

 V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)
       +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_r||^2.

The cited accepted identity states, under the original flow,

 V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.

Therefore any ACTUAL legal trajectory with V_h<0 excludes every
original nonnegative Lambda with full PSD M: the left side would be
negative and the right side nonnegative. No support selection is needed.

Here is a common complete construction for the planar witnesses.
Let K be a compact convex set containing0 in its interior and define

 F(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.

Completing the square shows that the unique maximizing g is the
Euclidean projection P_K(x). The two projection normal inequalities at
x and x' imply ||P_K(x)-P_K(x')||^2<=<P_K(x)-P_K(x'),x-x'>, hence the
projection is1-Lipschitz. The maximum formulas bound the differentiability
remainder between0 and ||P_K(x+z)-P_K(x)|| ||z||<=||z||^2. Thus F is
convex, differentiable and1-smooth, with grad F=P_K. It has unique
minimum F(0)=0: for x!=0 a sufficiently small positive epsilon has
epsilon x in K and gives F(x)>=epsilon(1-epsilon/2)||x||^2>0.

If the displayed g_i are projections, let y_i=x_i-g_i. Then
f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 and direct expansion gives

 Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j><=0

for EVERY ordered pair, by the projection normal inequality at j.
The optimum jet is x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Its two directions satisfy
Q_(i,star)=0 and Q_(star,i)=-<g_i,y_i><=0 because0 belongs to K.
This specifies all remote, terminal and optimum rows, not just adjacent
ones. Telescoping ||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2 gives

 V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_r+s g_r||^2.

In the constructions below x_r=g_r, so V_h=||x_0||^2-A^2||g_r||^2.
The unique minimizer is0, hence the true squared initial distance is
exactly ||x_0||^2 in every case.

The scalar screens used the interval version K=[-eta,1]: the derivative
is clip(x,[-eta,1]). At a selected step b>1, let P=1+prefix sum and
D=1+suffix sum, and choose eta=(b-1)/D. Start x_0=P. Gradients are1
before and at b, then -eta through the terminal point x_r=-eta.
Every point is on the claimed constant-gradient side of this SAME
function. Its energy is P^2-A^2(b-1)^2/D^2. Thus the original global
cut is A(b-1)<=PD. Steps<=1 contribute a vacuous nonnegative test.

The other scalar screens use the exact convex quadratic F_z(x)=z x^2/2,
z in{1/4,1/2,3/4,1}, x_0=1. With p_0=1 and
p_(i+1)=(1-z h_i)p_i, substitution gives

 V_h(z)=sum_i z h_i(1+z-z h_i)p_i^2
                 -s z[1+A z]p_r^2.

At z=1 this reduces to1-A^2 product_i(1-h_i)^2. Testing those four
curvatures makes no assertion about every curvature or nonlinear law.

For completeness, the adjacent-pair screen is also an original actual
function. Let the pair be b,c>1, put B=b-1,C=c-1,
P=1+prefix sum,D=1+suffix sum,U=b+c+D-1. If D>BC, set

 kappa=B^2 C+U(D-BC), t=B^2 C/kappa,
 u=(1,0), w=(t,sqrt(t(1-t))), v=-(B u+D w)/c,
 K=conv{u,v,w}.

Then0<t<1 and positive weights B,c,D express0 as an interior
convex combination. Prefix queries lie at positive multiples>=1 of u,
the two pair updates are u -> -B u -> D w, and suffix queries lie at
positive multiples>=1 of w. Their gradients are respectively u,v,w.
At -B u, with y=-B u-v, the exact comparisons are

 <w-v,y>=[-B^2 C+kappa t]/c^2=0,
 <u-v,y>=-B(1-t)C/c<0.

At a prefix normal a u, a>=0, both comparisons to v-u and w-u are
nonpositive. At a suffix normal a w, a>=0, the comparisons to u-w
and v-w are0 and -a t[1+(B+D)/c]<=0. Thus Section3's full row formula
applies and V_h=P^2-A^2 t, where A=P+U. Expansion gives

 A^2 B^2 C-P^2 kappa
       =U{BC[P^2+2BP+BU]-P^2 D}.

The resulting necessary inequality is P^2 D>=BC[P^2+2BP+BU].
The evaluator also contains a stop at D<=BC, recalled from the earlier
C80 draft. This document does not import that unverified branch: EVERY
applicable adjacent pair of EVERY registered word had D>BC, as the
retained exact raw outputs show. No D<=BC rejection occurred. Only the
fully proved D>BC branch was used in this finite batch or in this proof.

## 4. Exact scalar rejection of f5c

Take h=(3/2,5/2,23/20,12/5,27/20), A=99/10. At physical index1,
P=5/2,D=59/10 and eta=15/59. The scalar function is explicitly

 F(x)=x-1/2                    for x>=1,
 F(x)=x^2/2                    for -eta<=x<=1,
 F(x)=-eta*x-eta^2/2            for x<=-eta.

It is convex differentiable1-smooth, with unique minimizer0. The entire
trajectory is

 x=(5/2,1,-3/2,-285/236,-141/236,-15/59),
 g=(1,1,-15/59,-15/59,-15/59,-15/59).

Each listed update uses its original physical step, and every point
lies in the stated branch. The values are F(x_i); Section3 supplies
all original and optimum interpolation residuals. The true squared
distance is25/4. The global cut slack is

 PD-A(b-1)=(5/2)(59/10)-(99/10)(3/2)=-1/10.

The exact original primitive energy is

 V_h=(5/2)^2-(99/10)^2(15/59)^2
       =(295^2-297^2)/118^2=-296/3481<0.

Thus f5c cannot have any full original primitive certificate.

## 5. Exact planar rejection of f6c

Take h=(3/2,49/20,5/4,103/50,5/2,5/4), A=1201/100, and the
adjacent pair at physical indices3,4. The parameters of Section3 are

 P=31/5, B=53/50, C=3/2, D=9/4, U=581/100,
 D-BC=33/50, kappa=138/25, t=2809/9200.

Use u,w,v,K,F as in Section3; here v=-(53/125)u-(9/10)w.
The COMPLETE original trajectory and gradients are

 x=((31/5)u,(47/10)u,(9/4)u,u,-(53/50)u,(9/4)w,w),
 g=(u,u,u,u,v,w,w).

Every update is exact. Section3 checks each projection normal, every
ordered interpolation row and both optimum directions. Its unique
minimum is0, with true squared initial distance961/25. The adjacent
pair slack is -2660367/500000. More directly, the actual energy is

 V_h=961/25-(1201/100)^2(2809/9200)
       =-515224409/92000000<0.

This excludes every original42-entry/fullR7 certificate for f6c.

## 6. Exact rejection of the screen-passing word f5a

This is a textual counterexample construction motivated by a numerical
rejection, not a reconstruction of floating dual coefficients. Take

 h=(3/2,12/5,6/5,12/5,7/5), s=89/10, A=99/10,
 u=(1,0), v=(-2/5,3/10), w=(3/37,-839/4218),
 p=w+u+v, q=w+v, K=conv{p,q,w}.

The exact scalar products are

 ||u||^2=1, ||v||^2=1/4, <u,v>=-2/5,
 <w,u>=3/37, <w,v>=-7/76.

Put alpha=1165/6327 and beta=4195/6327. Coordinate substitution gives
w=-alpha u-beta v. The three barycentric coefficients alpha,
beta-alpha=3030/6327 and1-beta=2132/6327 are positive, so0 is interior
to K. Section3 makes F(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2) a single
globally convex differentiable1-smooth function with unique minimum0.

Let P=39/10, H=15/2 and start at

 x_0=A w+P u+H v=(63/37,987/3515).

Use the original five steps and propose g=(p,p,q,q,w,w). Directly
subtracting P p+(18/5)q+(7/5)w from x_0 ends at x_5=w. The normals
y_i=x_i-g_i are specified by

 y_1=(37/5)w+(7/5)u+5v,       y_0=y_1+(3/2)p,
 y_3=(19/5)w+(7/5)v,         y_2=y_3+(6/5)q,
 y_4=(7/5)w,                y_5=0.

Their complete two-direction table is

| i | g_i | <y_i,u> | <y_i,v> |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | p | 189/185 | -337/950 |
| 1 | p | 0 | 4/475 |
| 2 | q | -587/925 | 18/95 |
| 3 | q | -233/925 | 0 |
| 4 | w | 21/185 | -49/380 |
| 5 | w | 0 | 0 |

At p the necessary vertex comparisons are <y,u>>=0 and
<y,u+v>>=0. Row0's sum is23441/35150>0; row1's sum is4/475>0.
At q they are <y,u><=0 and <y,v>>=0, both satisfied in rows2,3.
At w they are <y,v><=0 and <y,u+v><=0; row4's sum is
-217/14060<0, and row5 is zero. Thus every proposed gradient is
indeed the projection at the corresponding physical iterate. All
original Q_ij and optimum rows follow from Section3, with no suppressed
sample or identification of formal gradient variables in the certificate.

The true squared initial distance is exactly
(63/37)^2+(987/3515)^2>0. The actual primitive energy telescopes to

 V_h=||A w+P u+H v||^2-A^2||w||^2
    =P^2-(4/5)PH+H^2/4+2A[(3/37)P-(7/76)H]
    =2349/400-1042767/140600
    =-434187/281200<0.

Consequently f5a is exactly excluded from the entire original30-entry,
fullR6 certificate cone, even with arbitrary nonzero PSD remainder.
This strengthens this ONE row beyond its numerical status.

For comparison, its complete original global-cut slacks were
(69/20,57/50,1077/50,39/50,227/50)>0. Its unit-curvature slack was
15530299/39062500>0. Every adjacent-pair test and all four registered
quadratic energies also passed, with exact data retained in f5a_raw.json.
Passing those necessary tests therefore does not ensure full feasibility.

## 7. Exact rejection of the screen-passing six-step word f6a

Take

 h=(3/2,5/2,5/4,39/20,5/2,131/100),
 s=1101/100, A=1201/100,
 u=(1,0), v=(-3/10,2/5), w=(22/317,-31243/161036),
 p=w+u+v, q=w+v, K=conv{p,q,w}.

Here

 ||u||^2=1, ||v||^2=1/4, <u,v>=-3/10,
 <w,u>=22/317, <w,v>=-25/254.

Set alpha=49025/644144 and beta=312430/644144. Direct coordinates
give w=-alpha u-beta v, and the three barycentric weights are
alpha, beta-alpha=263405/644144, and1-beta=331714/644144, all positive.
Thus0 is interior to K. Use the same complete construction of F from
Section3, with its unique minimizer0 and original interpolation rows.

Put P=4,H=97/10 and start at x_0=A w+P u+H v. Its first coordinate
is A*(22/317)+109/100>0, so the true squared initial distance
||x_0||^2 is positive. The proposed full gradient sequence is

 (g_0,...,g_6)=(p,p,q,q,q,w,w).

The total weights on p,q,w in the six physical updates are respectively
4,57/10,131/100. Therefore x_6=w exactly. The original normals are

 y_1=(951/100)w+(3/2)u+(36/5)v, y_0=y_1+(3/2)p,
 y_4=(381/100)w+(3/2)v,
 y_3=y_4+(39/20)q, y_2=y_4+(16/5)q,
 y_5=(131/100)w, y_6=0.

These formulas specify all original queries by x_i=g_i+y_i and use
every original step. To check the projection cone at p, the exact
first-switch normal satisfies

 <y_1,u>=0, <y_1,v>=2103/5080>0.

Also <p,u>=2439/3170>0 and
<p,u+v>=13/20+22/317-25/254>11/20>0, since25/254<1/10.
Thus both p rows satisfy <y_i,u>>=0 and <y_i,u+v>>=0.

At q the final-switch normal satisfies

 <y_4,u>=-5883/31700<0, <y_4,v>=0.

Moreover <q,u>=-731/3170<0 and <q,v>=77/508>0. Adding the
nonnegative multiples of q in y_2,y_3 preserves both q cone tests:
<y_i,u><=0 and <y_i,v>>=0.

At w, <w,v>=-25/254<0 and

 <w,u+v>=22/317-25/254=-2337/80518<0.

Thus y_5 and y_6 satisfy both w cone tests. Every gradient is now
proved to be the actual projection at its physical iterate. Section3
gives every original ordered Q and both optimum rows without further
assumptions. The values are f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.

The exact energy is

 V_h=||A w+P u+H v||^2-A^2||w||^2
    =P^2-(3/5)PH+H^2/4
                         +2A[(22/317)P-(25/254)H]
    =6497/400-130958241/8051800
    =-353759/16103600<0.

This excludes every original42-entry/fullR7 certificate for f6a.
It is an additional actual-function obstruction beyond all registered
global cuts, adjacent-pair tests and scalar quadratic screens, which
this word passed. No numerical infeasibility theorem is used to obtain
the conclusion.


## 8. What remains

No actual full seed at the accepted capacities has been constructed.
Only the eight listed proposals were tested, and only f5a,f5c,f6a,f6c have
the complete exact rejections supplied here. The numerical ledger is
CLOSED. The unrestricted original Goal is still OPEN, and nothing here
is a new accepted fact. No formal gate or additional computation is
authorized by this document.

One already allocated analytical round remains after this handoff. A
useful next textual question is whether the explicit three-vertex law
in Sections6--7 extend to the other screen-passing five/six-step words,
or whether its original normal inequalities expose a concrete direction
for a future seed construction. Such work must first re-read TASK.md
and the latest guidance and may not restart this numerical batch.
