# Exact scalar value pricing and its sharp same-sign redundancy

Status: COMPLETE JOINT PROOF DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. max2 round24, the second
and final analytical round authorized after baseline22 by TASK.md and
f7608aa358751108. This does not create a new fact or use an earlier draft
as an accepted premise. No numerical/model evaluation, scan, solver,
replay, machine algebra, verifier, installation, or subagent is used.

Accepted inputs: 04219554e41d9750, Section10, supplies the ORIGINAL full
primitive certificate identity. 1252b8823bb5a290, proof paragraphs1--2,
supplies the smooth-convex interpolation convention and its necessity.
The new arguments below are elementary and included in full.

The concrete outcome is exact redundancy of value pricing on EVERY
one-dimensional GD trajectory whose sampled gradients have one sign.
It includes the entire matched quadratic-curvature regime with no sign
change, arbitrary nonnegative words, zero steps, repeated gradients and
zero-gradient terminal samples. This is a larger class than a fixed
quadratic, and the redundancy holds at every length and capacity.
Section8 also gives an arbitrary-dimensional extension: the entire
componentwise monotone geometry class, including matched quadratic
trajectories with no sign-changing eigencomponent, stays redundant even
when ALL sampled values of nonseparable interpolants are allowed.

The assertion is NOT about sign-changing scalar histories, arbitrary
planar repaired cuts, general C82 pricing, or full-cone sufficiency.
The missing full four-step seed and original exact-profile goal are open.

## 1. Original objective, all coordinates, and the sharp statement

Let n>=0 and h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be any nonnegative finite word. Put

 P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, s=P_n, C=1+s.

For scalar positions x_i, gradients g_i, and values f_i, retain ALL
indices0,...,n and every ordered pair. The physical recursion is
x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i. The primitive energy and signed interpolation
residual are

 V=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)
       +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)g_i^2-sC g_n^2,
 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2.              (1)

The original array Lambda has Lambda_ij>=0 for i!=j, Lambda_ii=0,
L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, and outgoing-minus-incoming flow

 L^T e=omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T.              (2)

Here e is the (n+1)-entry all-ones column. With T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for
t<i, T_it=0 for t>i, and D=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<n,-s(s+2)), its full
matrix is M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2. The accepted identity is

 V+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g_0,...,g_n)>.     (3)

Gram denotes the matrix of gradient inner products and the bracket is
the entrywise matrix inner product. Full feasibility requires M positive
semidefinite on ALL original formal gradient coordinates. No
equal-gradient equation at a zero step is imposed on M. Sparse arrays
below solve only an auxiliary flow minimum and are never asserted to
be full PSD certificates.

Fix any scalar physical geometry with

 g_0>=g_1>=...>=g_n>=0,
 r_i=g_i-g_(i+1), Delta_i=x_i-x_(i+1)=h_i g_i,
 0<=r_i<=Delta_i.                                    (4)

Every actual scalar convex1-smooth GD history with nonnegative sampled
gradients satisfies(4). Indeed positions decrease and convexity makes
gradients decrease;1-Lipschitz continuity gives the last bound. Conversely
Section3 supplies one static actual function for every geometry(4).
Histories with all gradients nonpositive reduce to(4) by reflecting x
and negating g, which preserves every objective and residual in(1).

The exact minimum of V over ALL legal re-selections of the finite values
on this geometry is

 V_min=sum_(i<n)P_(i+1) r_i
              [2(1+P_i)g_i-P_(i+1)r_i].              (5)

It is attained by one explicit static convex1-smooth function with a
real minimizer, described in Section3. All optimum pairs are included.
For an arbitrary nonnegative word the following conditions are equivalent:

 (a) V>=0 for every one-dimensional actual convex1-smooth GD history
     with a real minimizer and sampled gradients of one sign;
 (b) h_i<=P_i+2 for every i<n.                         (6)

Moreover the pre-existing single-cut necessary tests for a full primitive
certificate imply the STRICT inequality h_i<P_i+2 for all i. Thus(5),
even optimized over all geometries(4), adds no constraint to those tests.
This redundancy uses a subset of the current complete necessary system;
intersecting further C82/C83, endpoint, or matrix-coupling conditions
cannot make the redundant test stronger.

## 2. Exact finite value optimum and every finite ordered pair

For each i<n define

 A_i=g_(i+1)Delta_i+r_i^2/2,
 u_i=Delta_i-r_i>=0.                                 (7)

The original ordered inequality Q_(i+1,i)<=0 gives
f_i-f_(i+1)>=A_i. Summing from i to n-1 yields
f_i-f_n>=sum_(k=i)^(n-1)A_k. All the objective weights h_i are
nonnegative. Hence every legal set of values has

 2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)
     >=2sum_(k<n)P_(k+1)A_k.                         (8)

Choose replacement values with precisely

 fhat_n=g_n^2/2,
 fhat_i=fhat_n+sum_(k=i)^(n-1)A_k.                    (9)

Their common additive constant is immaterial for(1). No independent
choices for different pairs or different runs are being combined.

For any original indices i<j, direct expansion of(9) gives BOTH
ordered residuals:

 Qhat_(j,i)=-sum_(i<=k<l<j)r_l u_k,
 Qhat_(i,j)=-sum_(i<=l<=k<j)r_l u_k.                  (10)

Here and below an empty sum is zero. To derive the first formula,
write g_(k+1)-g_j=sum_(l=k+1)^(j-1)r_l and expand
(g_i-g_j)^2=(sum_(k=i)^(j-1)r_k)^2. The square's cross terms cancel
the corresponding r_k r_l from Delta_k=r_k+u_k, leaving precisely the
first displayed sum. For the second, use
g_i-g_(k+1)=sum_(l=i)^k r_l in the same expansion; its diagonal terms
are retained, producing the second sum. Every summand is nonnegative,
so ALL distinct finite ordered pairs satisfy Qhat<=0.

Introduce a real optimum sample at

 x_star=x_n-g_n, g_star=0, fhat_star=0.                (11)

To check every optimum comparison, append one geometric interval after
index n with Delta_n=r_n=g_n and u_n=0. Its area A_n=g_n^2/2 equals
fhat_n. Formula(10) now applies verbatim with the appended optimum as
the last index. This artificial interval is ONLY an interpolation-pair
check; it is not an added GD step and carries no h weight or multiplier
in the original flow. It also works when g_n=0, so the optimum sample
coincides with x_n. Thus all(n+2)(n+1) ordered pairs, including optimum
pairs, have been checked with a single set of replacement values.

The construction in Section3 shows actual attainment. Therefore(8)
is an equality at the minimum over every finite legal value assignment,
including value assignments that originally came with a different
minimizer. A genuine minimizer for the selected assignment is explicit.

## 3. One static function and its true minimizer distance

Set m_0=x_n-g_n. Define one scalar derivative G on the entire real line.
For x<=x_n put G(x)=x-m_0. For each nonempty interval
[x_(i+1),x_i], in their spatial order from n-1 down to0, put

 G(x)=g_(i+1)+max{0,x-(x_i-r_i)}.                     (12)

Finally put G(x)=g_0 for x>=x_0. If Delta_i=0, (4) forces r_i=0;
the zero-length interval introduces no conflicting prescription. At a
shared endpoint both neighboring prescriptions give the supplied
gradient. Since 0<=r_i<=Delta_i, (12) first stays constant and then
increases with slope1. Consequently G is continuous, nondecreasing,
and1-Lipschitz on the whole real line.

Let

 F(x)=integral_(m_0)^x G(t) dt.                       (13)

This is one everywhere finite differentiable convex1-smooth function,
with derivative G. For x<x_star=m_0 its derivative is negative;
for x>=m_0 the derivative is nonnegative. Thus m_0 is a real minimizer
and F(m_0)=0. At x_n, F(x_n)=g_n^2/2. On each original interval,
integrating(12) gives exactly A_i. Therefore F(x_i)=fhat_i,
F'(x_i)=g_i, and every original GD update is realized by THIS F.

The complete minimizer set, and hence the real distance normalization,
can also be given. If g_n>0, then argmin F={m_0}. If g_n=0 but g_0>0,
let k be the largest original index with g_k>0. Then k<n and

 argmin F=[x_n,x_k-g_k].                             (14)

Indeed the interval from x_(k+1)=x_n up to x_k-g_k has derivative0,
and to its right(12) has positive derivative. To its left x<x_n has
negative derivative. All later zero-gradient steps leave the point
x_n fixed. In either nontrivial case the closest minimizer to x_0 is

 m_close=min_{i:g_i>0}(x_i-g_i),
 dist(x_0,argmin F)=x_0-m_close>0.                    (15)

For the formula, x_(i+1)-g_(i+1)=x_i-g_i-u_i is nonincreasing with
i; its smallest value among positive-gradient indices occurs at the
last such index. Formula(15) follows from(14) or the singleton case.
If g_0=0, all gradients and positions coincide, V=0, and the same
construction gives argmin F=[x_0,infinity); this trivial case has
zero initial distance and is not used as a normalized positive witness.

No unspecified external function or arbitrary initial radius enters.
Negative-gradient histories are obtained by reflecting the entire
function, including its actual minimizer set.

## 4. Original terminal-source flow and the attained slack price

Let f be ANY legal base value assignment on(4), and write
w_ij=-Q_ij>=0 for its cost on the directed ORIGINAL edge i->j.
In particular

 w_(i+1,i)=f_i-f_(i+1)-A_i.                          (16)

Put d_n=0 and d_i=sum_(k=i)^(n-1)w_(k+1,k). Choosing
zhat_i=-d_i subtracts exactly the excess in(16) from each adjacent
value difference. Thus f+zhat is(9) up to one common additive constant,
and is fully feasible by(10). Its feasibility says

 d_j-d_i<=w_ij for EVERY original ordered pair.       (17)

Summing(17) along any path from n to i makes its cost at least d_i.
The path n->n-1->...->i has cost d_i by definition. Hence d_i is
the EXACT original finite shortest-path distance, including zero costs,
duplicated points, and zero-gradient intervals. No optimum vertex is
needed to obtain this distance or flow.

The auxiliary flow with its only possibly nonzero entries

 Lambda_(i+1,i)=2P_(i+1),  0<=i<n,                   (18)

has the exact full original balance(2): at the terminal it has outgoing
mass2s; at node0 incoming mass2h_0; at every node0<i<n its outgoing
minus incoming mass is2P_i-2P_(i+1)=-2h_i. For n=0 the array is empty
and the entire assertion reads zero equals zero.

Multiplying(17) by ANY nonnegative array with balance(2) gives

 sum Lambda_ij w_ij >= -sum omega_i d_i
                     =2sum_(i<n)h_i d_i.

Array(18) attains equality, since its cost is
2sum_(i<n)P_(i+1)w_(i+1,i)=2sum_(i<n)h_i d_i. Therefore

 Pprice=min_original_flows sum Lambda_ij w_ij
        =2sum_(i<n)P_(i+1)w_(i+1,i),
 min_values V=V(base)-Pprice.                        (19)

The source is the terminal, and each original queried index i<n is
a sink of mass2h_i. The zero-step sinks have mass zero, but their
vertices and formal coordinates have NOT been removed. Formula(18)
is an exact price optimizer, NOT a full PSD certificate.

If an additional fixed optimum is required, it may constrain a common
shift or sometimes decrease the available price. The no-improvement
conclusion in Section6 still holds, because restricting legal values
can only increase the minimum. In the concrete matched-curvature
family of Section7, the fixed optimum0 preserves the full price and
all original pairs, so the distinction is resolved there exactly.

## 5. Derivation of the complete optimized energy

Substitute A_i from(7) into equality in(8), and use
g_(i+1)=g_i-r_i. Set

 W_i=h_i(1+2P_i+h_i).

Then

 V_min=sum_(i<n)W_i(g_i^2-g_n^2)
       -2sum_(i<n)P_(i+1)h_i g_i r_i
       +sum_(i<n)P_(i+1)r_i^2.                      (20)

The coefficient subtraction is valid since sum_(i<n)W_i=s(s+1),
as follows by telescoping
W_i=P_(i+1)(P_(i+1)+1)-P_i(P_i+1).
Also g_i^2-g_n^2=sum_(k=i)^(n-1)(2g_k r_k-r_k^2).
Interchanging these FINITE algebraic sums in(20) and using
sum_(i<=k)W_i=P_(k+1)(P_(k+1)+1) gives

 V_min=sum_(i<n)P_(i+1)
              [2(1+P_i)g_i r_i-P_(i+1)r_i^2],

which is(5). This derivation is exact and involves no computation or
assumption of a quadratic objective.

## 6. Sharp characterization and comparison with actual cut conditions

If h_i<=P_i+2, then P_(i+1)<=2(P_i+1). Since0<=r_i<=g_i,

 2(P_i+1)g_i-P_(i+1)r_i
   >=[P_i+2-h_i]g_i>=0.

Every summand in(5) is nonnegative. Every other legal value assignment
has V>=V_min, proving(b) implies(a) in(6).

Conversely suppose h_j>P_j+2 at an original index j. Put P=P_j,
b=h_j, m=b-1>0. Define a single derivative

 G(x)=0 for x<=m,
 G(x)=x-m for m<=x<=m+1,
 G(x)=1 for x>=m+1,

and F(x)=integral_m^x G(t)dt. This is convex differentiable1-smooth,
with argmin F=(-infinity,m]. Start at x_0=P+b. At every query i<=j,
x_i=P+b-P_i>=b=m+1 and g_i=1. Step j lands at x_(j+1)=0<=m;
all subsequent gradients are0 and subsequent iterates stay0. Thus
there is just one nonzero r, namely r_j=1. The function realizes the
optimized values of Section2 on all original samples. Formula(5) gives

 V=(P+b)(P+2-b)<0,
 dist(x_0,argmin F)=P+1.                             (21)

All interpolation pairs are actual pairs of this F; alternatively(10)
checks the finite pairs, and interpolation necessity checks the pairs
with the real minimizer m. The single function and its true radius
prove(a) implies(b), including zero steps elsewhere.

For completeness the existing single-cut condition follows from an
equally explicit original witness. Choose any j with b=h_j>1 and put

 P=P_j, R=sum_(t>j)h_t, eta=(b-1)/(1+R)>0.

Let F have derivative clip(x,-eta,1) and minimum0 at0. Start at x_0=1+P.
For i<=j, x_i=1+P-P_i>=1 and g_i=1. After step j, points have
x_k=-eta*(1+sum_(t>=k)h_t), g_k=-eta for k>j. In particular
x_n=g_n=-eta. All data are from this ONE convex1-smooth function,
with unique minimizer0 and true initial radius1+P. Its samples satisfy
f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2.

Expanding x_(i+1)^2=x_i^2-2h_i x_i g_i+h_i^2 g_i^2 and summing
shows in general for such sample values that

 V=x_0^2-(x_n+s g_n)^2.

Therefore this cut function has V=(1+P)^2-C^2 eta^2. By the original
full identity(3), every full certificate must obey

 C(b-1)<=(1+P)(1+R).                                (22)

These are precisely the scalar single-cut inequalities used in the
current four-step complete conditions. Since C=1+P+b+R>1+R,
(22) implies b-1<1+P, or b<P+2. For b<=1 the same strict bound holds
because P>=0. Thus every word passing the scalar cut family automatically
passes the ENTIRE optimized same-sign class in(6).

No total capacity bound or unverified compact-domain reduction is needed.
In particular adding the conditions from554ec17f7f590bf0,
d3abee1b17da52da, C82, C83, or full-matrix2948d4cd3660413f cannot
make same-sign scalar pricing a new separator. The proof only needs
the actual cuts(22), reconstructed here, which those systems retain.

## 7. Concrete matched curvature, all values, fixed optimum and true radius

Choose0<kappa<=1 and put

 p_0=1, p_i=product_(t<i)(1-kappa h_t),
 x_i=p_i, g_i=kappa p_i, f_i^base=kappa p_i^2/2.      (23)

Assume kappa h_i<=1 for every i. Thus p_i>=0 decreases, and
r_i=kappa^2 h_i p_i=kappa Delta_i. All geometry(4) holds, including
the original zero-step indices and any zero-gradient tail. The base
function is the single scalar quadratic kappa x^2/2, with real unique
minimizer0 and initial radius1.

Its complete original pair costs are

 w_ij=kappa(1-kappa)(p_i-p_j)^2/2.                   (24)

For chronological neighbors the path of Section4 gives

 d_i=kappa(1-kappa)/2 * sum_(k=i)^(n-1)(p_k-p_(k+1))^2,
 Pprice=kappa(1-kappa)sum_(i<n)P_(i+1)(p_i-p_(i+1))^2.
                                                               (25)

The optimized values can retain the SAME optimum sample0 and the SAME
terminal value as the base:

 fhat_n=kappa p_n^2/2,
 fhat_i=fhat_n+sum_(k=i)^(n-1)A_k=f_i^base-d_i.       (26)

To prove that no optimum pair is missed, use this explicit derivative:
put G(x)=kappa x for x<=p_n; use(12) on every interval
[p_(i+1),p_i]; and set G(x)=kappa for x>=1. Every piece meets its
neighbors continuously, with slopes kappa,0,1,or0. Consequently it is
nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz, and F(x)=integral_0^x G(t)dt realizes
ALL data(26) and the fixed optimum(0,0,0). All original ordered pairs
then follow either from the finite formula(10) or from interpolation
necessity in accepted1252b8823bb5a290. This is one fixed F during GD.

When p_n>0 its unique minimizer is0 and its true initial radius is1.
When p_n=0, let k be the largest index with p_k>0. Then k<n and

 argmin F=[0,(1-kappa)p_k],
 dist(1,argmin F)=1-(1-kappa)p_k>0.                  (27)

The proof is the zero-tail argument from Section3, with derivative
kappa x<0 for x<0. At kappa=1 the minimizer interval reduces to{0}.
Thus no changed closest-minimizer distance is silently replaced by1.

Equations(24)--(26), together with the original lower bound from all
finite flows, show that the original finite price and the fixed-optimum
price agree exactly in this whole family. The optimized energy is

 V_min=kappa^3 sum_(i<n)P_(i+1)h_i p_i^2
                  [2(1+P_i)-kappa P_(i+1)h_i].      (28)

This follows from(5) and r_i=kappa h_i g_i; explicitly
P_(i+1)r_i g_i=kappa^3 P_(i+1)h_i p_i^2.
For words passing(22), h_i<P_i+2 and kappa h_i<=1 make every bracket
positive at an active step, because
2(1+P_i)-kappa P_(i+1)h_i>=P_i+2-h_i>0.
Hence this ENTIRE continuum of curvature values, fully optimized over
all sampled values and retaining the fixed optimum, gives no additional
condition beyond(22). A positive price at kappa<1 is not a new separator.

## 8. Vector extension: all values on componentwise monotone geometry

The same conclusion holds for an explicitly defined vector class, even
when the value assignments are allowed to come from NONSEPARABLE smooth
convex functions. Fix a finite dimension d and one orthonormal basis.
Independent reflections of its coordinates are allowed once at the start.
Assume that in the resulting basis every coordinate q obeys

 g_(0,q)>=...>=g_(n,q)>=0,
 0<=r_(i,q):=g_(i,q)-g_(i+1,q)<=Delta_(i,q):=h_i g_(i,q).
                                                               (29)

The positions obey the original vector GD recursion. For vector jets
the adjacent original interpolation inequality gives

 f_i-f_(i+1)>=A_i^vec,
 A_i^vec=sum_(q=1)^d
       [g_(i+1,q)Delta_(i,q)+r_(i,q)^2/2].            (30)

Summing these inequalities with weights2P_(i+1) proves the lower bound
for EVERY full legal value assignment, without a separability hypothesis
on its interpolating function. Apply the scalar construction(12)--(13)
to each coordinate and define the ONE function

 F_vec(x)=sum_(q=1)^d F_q(x_q).                       (31)

Its gradient is1-Lipschitz in Euclidean norm because each coordinate
derivative is1-Lipschitz and squared differences add. Convexity and
every GD equation are coordinatewise. All finite and optimum residuals
are sums of the nonpositive scalar residuals(10). The original values
of F_vec attain equality in(30) simultaneously. Therefore the minimum
over ALL legal values, including values of coupled functions, is

 V_min^vec=sum_(q=1)^d sum_(i<n)P_(i+1)r_(i,q)
             [2(1+P_i)g_(i,q)-P_(i+1)r_(i,q)].       (32)

For an arbitrary legal base on this geometry, exactly the same ORIGINAL
flow Lambda_(i+1,i)=2P_(i+1) attains the slack price. The proof of(17)
uses feasibility of the attaining separable values and therefore applies
to the complete vector residuals, not only to their individual scalar
costs. The optimum of a sum of costs is attained by this COMMON path;
separate incompatible paths or functions have not been combined.

The minimizer set of(31) is the Cartesian product of the scalar sets
in Section3. Its closest point to x_0 is the vector of coordinatewise
closest minimizers, and its TRUE squared initial distance is the sum
of their squared distances. Coordinates with every gradient zero
contribute zero energy and zero distance for the constructed function.
There is a real minimizer and the product function is static throughout.

Condition h_i<=P_i+2 makes every term in(32) nonnegative. Necessity for
nonnegativity over this entire vector class already follows from the
scalar witness(21). Consequently the sharp characterization(6) and
redundancy under the actual cuts(22) extend to ALL geometries(29).
Coordinatewise monotonicity AND the upper decrement bound in(29) are
substantive; keeping gradients in one orthant alone does not imply them.

A concrete subfamily uses a real symmetric matrix A with eigenvalues
0<kappa_q<=1, base function x^T A x/2, and any initial vector. If
h_i kappa_q<=1 for every i,q, choose the orthonormal eigenbasis and
reflect each coordinate so its initial entry v_q is nonnegative. Then

 x_(i,q)=v_q product_(t<i)(1-kappa_q h_t),
 g_(i,q)=kappa_q x_(i,q),

so(29) holds. If v_q>0, set F_q(x)=v_q^2 F_unit(x/v_q), where F_unit
is the scalar construction of Section7 at curvature kappa_q. This gives
an attaining product function with the SAME fixed optimum0 and ALL
original pairs. At v_q=0 choose a constant zero coordinate function.
Thus the exact original price agrees with the fixed-optimum price and
is the sum of the expressions(25), each multiplied by v_q^2.

If every active component has a positive terminal product, the true
distance is ||x_0||, because every active coordinate has unique
minimizer0 and inactive zero coordinates start at0. If a component
first hits0, its closest minimizer shifts as in(27); the true squared
distance is the sum of v_q^2 times the squared scalar factors from(27).
Every value-reselection in this ENTIRE arbitrary-dimensional quadratic
geometry family therefore adds no full-cone condition beyond the
existing scalar cuts. No restriction on alternative interpolating
functions was imposed during the value minimization.

## 9. Scope, comparison and remaining interfaces

The assertion in this document is stronger than redundancy for a single
matched-curvature curve: (6) decides ALL same-sign scalar trajectories,
without a prescribed curvature or value law. Its converse(21) proves
the prefix-step threshold is exact for this class. Its price(19) is an
attained original-flow minimum, and(13)/(26) are explicit actual static
functions. This is not an unresolved potential or dual formulation.

The previous unit-curvature planar repaired-cut rigidity and2948 lifted
geometry rigidity are different, already published joint drafts. Their
proofs are not prerequisites here. C82 has a first sign reversal and a
transverse gradient; the same-sign proof cannot be applied to that
family. In particular the strict complementary formula in
e9ef65394000dcb9 remains to be compared with all the other full conditions.
Curvature choices making some p_i change sign are likewise outside(23)'s
stated parameter regime. No arbitrary-curvature redundancy is claimed.

A direct scope counterexample rules out dropping the one-sign hypothesis.
For any single step b with sqrt(2)<b<2, use F(x)=x^2/2 and x_0=1.
Its actual terminal gradient is1-b<0, its unique minimizer is0, and
every original/optimum pair is legal. The prefix bound b<P_0+2=2
holds, but direct substitution gives

 V=1-(1+b)^2(1-b)^2=b^2(2-b^2)<0.                   (33)

This is the familiar unit-quadratic obstruction, reconstructed only as
a scope check. It is already rejected by the single-cut condition and
is NOT a new surviving-region exclusion. In particular(6) cannot be
used to assert a full certificate for every word passing its prefix
bounds, or to dismiss sign-changing curvature/value trajectories.

All full original interpolation multipliers and formal gradient
coordinates remain in(1)--(3). The physical scalar witness does not
restrict the dimensions allowed by the original cone. Excluding witness
classes does not prove that the cone is nonempty; excluding all seeds
would still decide only the sufficient negative route of04219554e41d9750,
not the original affirmative asymptotic minimax goal.

Literature grounding: a Matlas complete-statement interpolation query
returned unrelated results, including a nonconvex C^(1,1) interpolation
criterion which was not imported. Web fallback recovered the already
audited primary source Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur1502.05666. Its local
Theorem4 proof, constructive discussion, Corollary1 and cycle Remark3
were reread. No new external theorem is used as a proof input.

All conclusions are UNVERIFIED joint drafts. The specified zero verifier
allocation is respected. No target fact, seed, global no-seed theorem,
general cone reversal, or original-target solution is claimed.

# Growing geometric ellipsoids: retained state, an actual calibration, and the unpaid interface

Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED proof draft of the scoped statements below.
The originally selected family, allowing ALL initial vectors, remains BLOCKED.
In particular, the calibration tube proved weak below is NOT the entire family.
This is the ONE authorized round48, completed ceiling48/attempted49. No
mathematical computation, solver, replay, formal verifier, consult, or subagent
was used. No fact is claimed, and no additional round is requested or assumed.

Accepted correctness inputs are1252b8823bb5a290 (normalization and the original
all-pair interpolation convention), b98bb807d514e80d (K and the original
benchmark recurrences), 1fca3e5dd555cb4a (the complete ray reference), and
dfde2fa326c908bf (the specified full complex reference). All new ingredients
are derived jointly here. Earlier ellipsoid and reference drafts are awareness,
not premises. This is an H_m-family investigation, not the all-word target.

## 1. One growing body and the original physical word

Fix an integer m>=128. All vector norms and inner products are Euclidean. Put

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c,
    b_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    n=2^m-1, h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1))=H_m,
    S_j=sum H_j, S=S_m, M_j=1+S_j, M=M_m, T=2S+1,
    alpha=a/(c-2), Cax=c^m, D=Cax^2.

Every child uses this same enclosing m. Let e_0,...,e_m be the standard basis
of R^(m+1), and define the diagonal positive definite matrix and FILLED body

    d_l=c^(2l), 0<=l<=m, A=diag(d_0,...,d_m),
    E={g: <g,A g><=1},
    F(x)=max_(g in E) {<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.                 (1)

The squared semiaxis ratio is D=c^(2m), and dimension is m+1. The selected
full law allows EVERY x_0!=0 in this space. Orthogonal rotations or a common
rescaling produce equivalent instances but are unnecessary here. The actual
GD is x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, g_i=grad F(x_i), retaining all n original steps.
We write f_i=F(x_i) and P=2f_n/||x_0||^2. Section2 proves that the unique
minimizer is0, so this denominator is the true squared initial distance.
Multiplying F by any L>0 and using h_i/L preserves the trajectory and P.

The elementary mass identities used below are

    M_j=alpha c^j+(1-alpha)2^j,
    0<alpha<1, M_j<=c^j, M_(j+1)<=c M_j,
    a c^j<=(c-2)M_j.                                  (2)

Indeed S_(j+1)=2S_j+1+a c^j gives the formula by induction. Also
c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta, so alpha<1. The other inequalities follow
from c>2 and the two nonnegative terms in the mass formula. We shall use

    12/5<lambda<c<5/2, 3/8<a<1/2, Cax>16, S>1248.       (3)

For the upper bound on c, sqrt(2)<17/12 gives
c<(29/12)(129/128)<5/2. Then a=beta/c>(127/128)(2/5)>3/8.
The lower bound lambda>12/5 follows from sqrt(2)>7/5. The remaining bounds
follow from m>=128 and S>=2^m-1.

## 2. Exact static geometry, smoothness, minimum, and every original row

Completing the square in (1) identifies its unique maximizing vector g(x)
with the Euclidean projection of x onto E. Existence is by compactness;
uniqueness follows by strict convexity of squared distance on a convex set.
For x in E set t(x)=0 and g(x)=x. For x outside E let t(x)>0 be the unique root

    sum_(l=0)^m d_l x_l^2/(1+t(x)d_l)^2=1,
    g_l(x)=x_l/(1+t(x)d_l).                            (4)

The sum on the left is continuous, strictly decreasing for nonzero x,
larger than1 at zero, and tends to0. For the resulting g, <g,A g>=1 and

    <x-g,z-g>=t(<g,A z>-1)<=0, z in E,

by Cauchy--Schwarz in the A inner product. Expanding squared distances
proves that this g is the projection, including coordinates equal to zero.
There are no negative multipliers: projection onto the boundary SURFACE
would be a different problem for interior points.

For two projection pairs (x,g),(y,p), adding their normal inequalities gives
||g-p||^2<=<g-p,x-y>. Thus g is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maxima defining
F(x+u) and F(x) shows that
0<=F(x+u)-F(x)-<g(x),u><=||u||^2. Therefore F is differentiable with gradient
g, is1-smooth, and is convex as a maximum of affine functions. Integrating
the Lipschitz gradient along a segment also gives the sharper upper estimate
F(x+u)<=F(x)+<g(x),u>+||u||^2/2, used in Section5.

The feasible vector0 gives F>=0 and F(0)=0. For x!=0, a sufficiently small
epsilon in (0,2) has epsilon*x in E and yields the strictly positive test
value epsilon(1-epsilon/2)||x||^2. Hence argmin F={0}.

Write t_i=t(x_i), including the optimum sample x_*=g_*=t_*=f_*=0. Exactly,

    x_i=(I+t_i A)g_i,
    f_i=||g_i||^2/2+t_i,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =t_j(<g_j,A g_i>-<g_j,A g_j>)<=0.              (5)

For t_j=0 the expression is zero. Otherwise <g_j,A g_j>=1 and its other
inner product is at most1. Thus EVERY ordered adjacent, remote, and optimum
row of the original interpolation system holds, without selecting a subset.
For two exterior samples, Q_ij=-(t_j/2)||g_i-g_j||_A^2, where
||v||_A^2=<v,A v>. These formulas apply to the same single F throughout.

The query is genuinely coupled. At an exterior point put
J(x)=sum_l d_l^2 x_l^2/(1+t d_l)^3>0. Implicit differentiation of (4) gives

    partial t/partial x_l=[d_l x_l/(1+t d_l)^2]/J(x),
    partial g_k/partial x_l
      =-[d_k x_k/(1+t d_k)^2][d_l x_l/(1+t d_l)^2]/J(x), k!=l. (6)

This is nonzero when both coordinates are active. The denominators have
the SAME changing multiplier. In the core the Hessian is I. Outside, the
identity <g,A g>=1 forces (A g)^T Dg=0, so its Hessian is singular. The
function is not a fixed quadratic, nor a sum of independent scalar modes.

## 3. An exact global state law with no loss of core amplitude

At each query retain ALL the weights

    w_i,l=d_l g_i,l^2, sigma_i=sum_l w_i,l,
    G_i=sum_l w_i,l/d_l=||g_i||^2,
    R_i^2=sum_l w_i,l(1+t_i d_l)^2/d_l=||x_i||^2.       (7)

They satisfy w_i,l>=0, sigma_i<=1, t_i>=0 and t_i(1-sigma_i)=0. In the
core t_i=0, but sigma_i and G_i retain both A radius and Euclidean radius;
they are NOT replaced by zero or by a fresh boundary point.

For a physical step h_i define

    u_i,l=w_i,l(1-h_i+t_i d_l)^2, U_i=sum_l u_i,l.      (8)

If U_i<=1, then t_(i+1)=0 and w_(i+1),l=u_i,l. If U_i>1, the next multiplier
is the unique positive solution of

    sum_l u_i,l/(1+t_(i+1)d_l)^2=1,
    w_(i+1),l=u_i,l/(1+t_(i+1)d_l)^2.                  (9)

Indeed sqrt(d_l)x_(i+1),l=(1-h_i+t_i d_l)sqrt(d_l)g_i,l, and applying (4)
to this ACTUAL next position gives (8)--(9). Signs, if a full vector
transcript is wanted, are carried by multiplication by
sign(1-h_i+t_i d_l); a zero coordinate stays zero. Formula (9) is therefore
an exact iteration, not an independent-coordinate relaxation.

An initial state is any t_0>=0 and w_0,l>=0 satisfying the stated
complementarity, except the all-zero state. Choosing signs and setting
x_0,l=(1+t_0 d_l)sign_l sqrt(w_0,l/d_l) realizes exactly that state by (4).
Conversely every initial vector has such a state. The global payoff is

    P=[G_n+2t_n]/R_0^2.                               (10)

The exact radius payment over ANY original prefix of length k is

    R_k^2=R_0^2-sum_(i<k) h_i(2-h_i)G_i
                   -2sum_(i<k) h_i t_i.              (11)

Expand ||x_i-h_i g_i||^2 and use <x_i,g_i>=G_i+t_i. Thus all normal work
and every negative coefficient from h_i>2 remain in (11). Dropping either
the weights at a core entry or those negative coefficients changes the
problem. Equations (7)--(11) are global for arbitrary initial vectors and
arbitrary eccentricity; no kappa<=2 drainage estimate was invoked.

## 4. A same-word counterexample to closing the state with two scalars

The pair (normal multiplier, Euclidean radius) does NOT determine even
the first transition of the selected law. Take the SAME E and F, and

    X_0=(1+1/Cax)e_0, Y_0=(1+1/Cax)e_m.                (12)

Both have t_0=1/Cax, radius1+1/Cax, and sigma_0=1. Their first original
step b_0=1+a gives

    X_1=(1/Cax-a)e_0 in E,  t(X_1)=0,
    Y_1=(1-a/Cax)e_m outside E,
    t(Y_1)=(Cax-1-a)/D>0.                             (13)

The distinction is their weights, concentrated respectively at l=0 and
l=m. It arises at the actual first query, not at an unrealized block state.

Both entire H_m trajectories can be given exactly. Let q_j be the product
of all positive step excesses b_l-1 in H_j. Induction from
q_(j+1)=q_j^2 a c^j gives

    q_j=beta^(2^j-1)c^(-j).                            (14)

Every nonempty prefix product in H_j is at most a: a prefix in the left
copy has this bound inductively; the product through the bridge is
q_j a c^j=a beta^(2^j-1)<=a; and continuing into the right copy multiplies
it by a prefix product at most1. Consequently each prefix product of the
tail obtained by deleting the FIRST physical step is at most1. Since X_1
is in E, every remaining X query stays in E, and

    P_X=(q_m/a)^2(a-1/Cax)^2/(1+1/Cax)^2.               (15)

For Y, (2) gives S<Cax-1. Hence all proposed positions
Y_i=(Cax+1-sum_(k<i)h_k)e_m/Cax are strictly exterior, have projection
e_m/Cax, and satisfy the actual recursion. Thus

    P_Y=[1+2(Cax-S)]/(Cax+1)^2.                        (16)

They have the same initial pair in (12), but different entire trajectories.
This does not refute an H_m-specific payoff bound. It refutes the proposed
scalar-state closure, even if sigma=1 is also recorded initially. The
counterexample explains why the full weights in Section3 are load-bearing.

## 5. A fully coupled calibration with a quantitative true-radius gap

Define the explicit finite amplification bound

    J_m=product_(j=0)^(m-1) max(1,a c^j)^(2^(m-1-j)).   (17)

It is a symbolic product, not a computed trajectory or an evaluation.
Consider the ENTIRE following tube of initial vectors for the same body:

    x_0=(T/Cax)e_m+z, <z,e_m>=0,
    ||z||<=1/(4Cax J_m).                              (18)

In particular z=(sum_(l=0)^(m-1)e_l)/(4Cax J_m sqrt(m)) has every coordinate
nonzero. The body still has all m+1 scales. We prove, uniformly over (18),

    1/[T(1+1/(16J_m^2 T^2))] <= P
          <= (1/T)(1+9/(16T)),
    R_0^2=T^2/D+||z||^2,
    T/D <= 2F(x_n) <= (T+9/16)/D.                     (19)

This is a weak calibration family, not a restriction imposed on the full
law from Section1. Its tube width can be extremely small. That limitation
is essential to its obstruction and cannot be omitted in a future citation.

Proof. For z=0, every query is exterior and

    x_i^b=(T-P_i)e_m/Cax, P_i=sum_(k<i)h_k,
    g_i^b=e_m/Cax, t_i^b=(2S-P_i)/D,
    2F(x_n^b)=T/D.                                   (20)

For (18), |g_i,m|<=1/Cax always. Summing actual steps implies
x_i,m>=(T-P_i)/Cax>1/Cax for every i<=n. Thus every actual query and the
terminal point is exterior as well. This validates the regime globally.

The map U_h(x)=x-h P_E(x) has Lipschitz constant at most max(1,h-1) for
h>=0. To prove this, set delta=x-y and v=P_E(x)-P_E(y). Firm
nonexpansiveness gives <delta,v>>=||v||^2, so
||delta-hv||^2<=||delta||^2+h(h-2)||v||^2. If h<=2 this is at most
||delta||^2. If h>=2 use ||v||<=||delta|| to obtain (h-1)^2||delta||^2.
All these factors are at least1, so each physical prefix satisfies

    ||x_i-x_i^b||<=J_m||z||<=1/(4Cax).                (21)

At the terminal point the feasible gradient e_m/Cax in (1) and
x_n,m>=x_n,m^b show F(x_n)>=F(x_n^b). Smoothness from Section2 gives

    F(x_n)<=F(x_n^b)+(1/Cax)||x_n-x_n^b||
                          +||x_n-x_n^b||^2/2
           <=F(x_n^b)+9/(32D).

Orthogonality in (18) gives the exact initial radius and (19).

The displayed dense choice actually changes gradient direction. From (4),
t_0<=sqrt(sum_l x_0,l^2/d_l)<=T/D+||z||<9/(4Cax)<a. Its l=0 coordinate
therefore changes sign at the first step, because 1-b_0+t_0=t_0-a<0.
Its l=m coordinate stays positive by the preceding exterior proof. Both
coordinates of g_0 are positive, whereas g_1 has these different signs.
The two gradients are not parallel, and (6) is nonzero initially. The
calibration is an actual nonlinear coupled trajectory, despite being
quantitatively close to the axial Huber trajectory.

## 6. Exact local departure law: the missing feedback is a positive term

The tube obstruction does not prove that a larger transverse displacement
fails. Here is an exact second variation at its axial center, retaining
the nonlinear feedback instead of freezing its effect on the slow axis.
For each transverse l<m define

    r_l=d_l/D, v_i,l=1/[1+r_l(2S-P_i)],
    p_0,l=1, p_(i+1),l=(1-h_i v_i,l)p_i,l,
    V_l=v_n,l p_n,l^2+r_l sum_(i<n) h_i v_i,l^2 p_i,l^2. (22)

All P_i and h_i are the ORIGINAL chronological data. These formulas are
not the fixed-curvature polynomial modes of an independent quadratic.
For fixed m and arbitrary transverse z tending to0, the actual objective is

    2F(x_n)=T/D+sum_(l<m) V_l z_l^2+O_m(||z||^4),
    P=1/T+(D/T^2)sum_(l<m)(V_l-1/T)z_l^2+O_m(||z||^4). (23)

The constants implicit in O_m can depend on m; no uniform nonlinear
conclusion is inferred from this expansion.

Proof. Every baseline query has t_i^b>0. The derivative in t in (4) is
nonzero there, so the usual implicit differentiation proof (or its local
power series) makes the projection analytic in a neighborhood of each
of the finitely many baseline queries. Compose these finitely many maps.
Reflection symmetry in each transverse coordinate makes its own output
coordinate odd in z_l and all other output coordinates even in z_l. Thus

    x_i,l=p_i,l z_l+O_m(||z||^3),
    g_i,l=v_i,l p_i,l z_l+O_m(||z||^3).

The boundary equation, with the positive last gradient coordinate, gives

    g_i,m=(1/Cax)sqrt(1-sum_(l<m)d_l g_i,l^2)
          =1/Cax-(1/(2Cax))sum_(l<m)d_l v_i,l^2 p_i,l^2 z_l^2
                       +O_m(||z||^4).

Hence

    x_n,m-x_n,m^b=(1/(2Cax))sum_(l<m)
          [d_l sum_(i<n) h_i v_i,l^2 p_i,l^2]z_l^2+O_m(||z||^4).

At a baseline point the Hessian
of F has transverse diagonal entries v_i,l and last entry0, as follows
from (4); all mixed entries there are zero. Taylor expansion at the
terminal baseline therefore gives (22)--(23). In particular the sum in
V_l is the actual second-order DELAY in the last coordinate. Omitting it
would compute a different dynamical system.

If V_l>1/T for any l, sufficiently small nonzero displacement in that
coordinate beats the axial Huber value at this fixed m. If all V_l<1/T,
the axis is a strict local maximum for that fixed m. Neither observation
supplies a benchmark-scale gain. A growing fast range and the nearest slow
transverse axis can be screened analytically; the intermediate range and finite displacements
are not settled.

### 6.1 Fast-axis local obstruction, including a growing number of axes

For every l<m with

    d_l <= Cax/[8(5/4)^m],                             (24)

one has V_l<1/T. This set contains l=0 and grows with m.

Indeed 2S r_l<=2d_l/Cax<1, so 1/2<v_i,l<=1. Since h_i>=1+a>4/3,
both signs give |1-h_i v_i,l|<=h_i-1. Thus every |p_i,l| is bounded by
the corresponding product of positive unit-curvature excesses.
Let E_j be sum h_i times the square of that prefix product over H_j.
By (14) and concatenation,

    E_(j+1)=[1+(q_j a c^j)^2]E_j+b_j q_j^2
           <=(5/4)E_j+(3/2)(5/12)^j, E_0=0.

The inequality uses q_j a c^j<=a<1/2, q_j<=c^(-j), and c>12/5.
Summing the geometric upper bound gives
E_m<2(5/4)^m. Also p_n,l^2<=q_m^2<=1/D. Consequently

    V_l <= r_l E_m+1/D
         <1/(4Cax)+1/Cax^2<1/(2Cax)<1/T.

The last inequality follows from T=2M-1<2Cax. Condition (24) contains
all l with 2l log(c)<=m log(4c/5)-log(8), so its size tends to infinity.

### 6.2 The slow transverse neighbor is also a strict local loss

For l=m-1, again V_l<1/T. Put r=c^(-2), v_i=v_i,m-1 and p_i=p_i,m-1.
We have 1/6<r<1/5. A slightly sharper rational c bound is useful:
sqrt(2)<99/70 implies c<21801/8960<73/30. If b=b_(m-1), (2) yields

    S/b >= c/(c-2)-[c/(c-2)+1]/b > 28/5-7/b > 559/100,

because c/(c-2)>73/13>28/5, c/(c-2)+1<7, and b>700 by (3).
Every physical step has h_i<=b, and 2S-P_i>=S+h_i. Therefore

    0<h_i v_i<=c^2 h_i/(S+h_i)<=c^2 b/(S+b)<9/10.    (25)

For the last strict bound it suffices that
(5329/900)/(659/100)<9/10, equivalently 5,329,000<5,337,900.

The actual linked denominators satisfy v_(i+1)=v_i/(1-rh_i v_i). Direct
subtraction gives

    v_(i+1)p_(i+1)^2-v_i p_i^2
      =-h_i v_i^2 p_i^2 [2-r-h_i v_i]/[1-rh_i v_i]
      <=-(45/41)h_i v_i^2 p_i^2.                     (26)

The bracket decreases with h_i v_i: its derivative is
-(1-r)^2/(1-rh_i v_i)^2. At h_i v_i=9/10 the bracket is
(11/10-r)/(1-9r/10), decreasing in r, so it is at least45/41 for r<=1/5.
Telescoping (26) and using (22) gives

    V_(m-1) <= (41r/45)v_0+(1-41r/45)v_n p_n^2
             <41/(90S)+6 exp(-499/100)/S.             (27)

For the last term, (25) ensures positive factors and
p_n^2<=exp(-2sum h_i v_i)<=exp[-2S/(1+2rS)]<=exp(-499/100), since
S>1248 and r<1/5. Also v_n<1/(rS)<6/S.

For a rational bound, the first seven Taylor terms of exp(5/3) sum to
2,774,305/524,880>21/4. Moreover exp(1/100)<100/99 by its series. Hence
exp(499/100)>99(21/4)^3/100=916839/6400>143. Substitution into (27) gives

    V_(m-1) < [41/90+6/143]/S
             =6403/(12870S)<499/(1000S)<=1/(2S+1).

The last inequality uses S>1248. This proves the claimed local loss.
It does not sign all other V_l and does not control nonlocal initial states.

## 7. Comparison with the complete K and ray references on this word

Here K_n(h) has its COMPLETE accepted definition: the maximum of
1/(1+2sum h_i) and all (h_i-1)_+^2/[(1+P_i)^2(1+2T_i)], where
P_i=sum_(k<i)h_k and T_i=sum_(k>i)h_k. We reproduce directly that

    K_n(H_m)=F_ray_n(H_m)=1/T.                         (28)

This reference calculation is recalled from shared awareness but its
complete argument is given here; it is not an unverified predecessor.

For a fixed level step b_j, let p range over the preceding masses at its
occurrences. The reciprocal cut denominator is
(1+p)^2[1+2(S-b_j-p)]. Its derivative changes sign at most once, from
positive to negative. Its minimum over the interval from first to last
occurrence is therefore at an endpoint. For the first occurrence, p=S_j
and the following mass is at least S_(m-1). Write r_c=c-2 and N=M_(m-1).
Using a c^j<=r_c M_j bounds this cut by r_c^2/(2N-1). For the last
occurrence the preceding mass is at least S_(m-1), the following mass
equals S_j, and its cut is at most
r_c^2 M_j^2/[N^2(2M_j-1)]<=r_c^2/(2N-1). The last inequality follows
by differentiating x^2/(2x-1) on x>=1 and using M_j<=N. Finally

    r_c^2(2M-1)<=(5N-1)/4<=2N-1,

by c<5/2, r_c<1/2, M<=cN and N>=1. Thus every cut is at most1/T.

For completeness, the accepted ray reference maximizes over ALL ordered
checkpoint subsets. If the steps are split as
(block_0,b_0,block_1,...,b_(k-1),block_k), put s_i=sum block_i. For k>=1
its nonterminal magnitudes and offsets are
m_0=1/(1+s_0), O_0=0,
m_(i+1)=((b_i-1)m_i-O_i)/(1+s_(i+1)),
O_(i+1)=O_i+s_i m_i, i<k-1. Require m_i>0 and m_i>=m_(i-2) for i>=2.
Put z_term=(b_(k-1)-1)m_(k-1)-O_(k-1), m_old=0 if k=1 and otherwise
m_(k-2), and require z_term>=(1+s_k)m_old. With
e_term=sum_(i<k, i congruent k modulo2)s_i m_i^2, optimize
2e_term+2q z_term-(2s_k+1)q^2 over m_old<=q<=z_term/(1+s_k).
For k=0 the optimum is1/T. This is the reference from1fca3e5dd555cb4a,
with its remote offsets and function-value areas intact.

Any two checkpoint indices i<j of levels u,v have preceding and
intervening masses p,s. Put r_u=a c^u, r_v=a c^v. We claim

    r_u r_v<(1+p)(1+s).                               (29)

If i lies before the complete H_v just preceding j (in particular if
u>=v), local copies give 1+p>=M_u and 1+s>=M_v, and (29) follows from
r_u<=r_c M_u, r_v<=r_c M_v, r_c<1. Otherwise u<v and i lies inside that
H_v. Its local preceding mass p_loc satisfies
1+p_loc>=M_u, 1+s>=M_u and (1+p_loc)+(1+s)=M_v-r_u. Multiplying the two
nonnegative quantities after subtracting M_u gives
(1+p)(1+s)>=M_u(M_v-r_u-M_u). At v=u+1, the latter quantity minus r_u r_v
equals M_u^2-c r_u^2>0 since c r_c^2<5/8. At successive v its increment
is M_u M_v+[M_u-(c-1)r_u]r_v>0, since (c-1)r_c<3/4. This proves (29).

If a ray selects at least two checkpoints, its first two slopes and
second-return budget are
m_0=1/(1+p), m_1=r_u m_0/(1+s), O_1=p m_0,
r_v m_1-O_1=m_0[r_u r_v/(1+s)-p]<m_0. For exactly two checkpoints this
violates the required terminal old-slope lower bound. For three or more
it forces m_2<m_0 after division by1+s_2, violating same-ray monotonicity.
Thus only zero or one checkpoint is feasible, whose maximum is K. This
proves (28) for the entire ray reference.

Consequently the whole calibration tube satisfies the exact comparison

    K_n/[1+1/(16J_m^2 T^2)] <= P
         <=(1+9/(16T))K_n=(1+9/(16T))F_ray_n.           (30)

In particular it has no fixed relative gain over these complete references.
Whether a particular nonzero z produces a smaller or slightly larger
value than K is not decided by this two-sided estimate; (23) is the exact
local test, with the subranges (24) and l=m-1 proved losing.

## 8. The specified FULL complex reference, not a lens substitute

For a complex parameter zeta=u+i v define
Poly_0(zeta)=1, Poly_i(zeta)=product_(k<i)(1-h_k zeta),
xi_i=Poly_i(zeta), gamma_i=zeta Poly_i(zeta), xi_*=gamma_*=0.
Identify C with the real plane with <p,q>=Re(conj(p)q). Give edge j->i
weight q_ij=<gamma_j,xi_i-xi_j>+|gamma_i-gamma_j|^2/2.
For every directed simple cycle C on {*,0,...,n}, including2-cycles, put
Area_C=sum_(j->i)Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i) and
Energy_C=sum_(j->i)|xi_i-xi_j|^2. Its FULL legal set is

    D_h={zeta:(u-|zeta|^2)Energy_C>=2|v Area_C| for EVERY C}.

For zeta in D_h let ell_i be the maximum q-weight of a path i->*, allowing
the empty path at *, and set Gap_h(zeta)=-2ell_n. The reference in this
section is F_cx,n(h)=sup_(zeta in D_h)Gap_h(zeta), with its specified
initial point1, minimizer0 and radius1, exactly as in dfde2fa326c908bf.
No universal lens, selected finite parameters or closest-minimizer
renormalization replaces it.

A rigorous bound sufficient for a constant comparison is

    F_cx,n(H_m)>=(S+1)^2/T^3.                          (31)

Choose theta=1/T, a real legal parameter: all cycle areas are multiplied
by v=0 and theta-theta^2>=0. The scalar quadratic theta*x^2/2 realizes
these points and gradients. The accepted maximality of Gap gives
Gap_h(theta)>=theta Poly_n(theta)^2. All theta h_i are nonnegative with
sum S/T<1, so induction using (1-u)(1-v)>=1-u-v gives
Poly_n(theta)>=1-S/T=(S+1)/T, proving (31).

Thus the calibration obeys, against this COMPLETE specified reference,

    P/F_cx,n <= (1+9/(16T)) T^2/(S+1)^2
              <4(1+9/(16T)).                         (32)

This is only a constant upper comparison. It does NOT decide P<=F_cx,n
or P>F_cx,n. A sharp ordered comparison with the full complex reference,
as well as a useful benchmark-reaching ellipsoid, is absent from this
pilot. No equality for a universal lens is silently promoted to D_h.

## 9. Original all-split B comparison: the entire tube is below half

Retain the ORIGINAL recurrences of b98bb807d514e80d:
C_0=B_0=1 and
C_k=max_(i+j=k-1)Omega(C_i,C_j),
B_k=max_(i+j=k-1)G(C_i,B_j), with
Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2 and
G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2. Every split is retained.
We first prove the bound B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^(m+1) directly.

Put eta=2lambda-1=lambda(4-lambda) and Llin=(4x+lambda^2 y)/eta.
Its coefficients exceed1 and

    eta^2[Llin^2-(x+y)Llin-xy]
      =(2x/lambda-sqrt(2)lambda*y)^2>=0.

Therefore Llin>=Omega(x,y). At z=lambda Omega(x,y), the polynomial with
larger root G(x,lambda y) has value
z^2-(4x+lambda y)z+4x^2=x[4x+lambda^2 y-eta Omega(x,y)]>=0.
Also z>=lambda(x+y)>=(4x+lambda y)/2, the vertex. Hence
G(x,lambda y)<=lambda Omega(x,y). Induction in the original recurrences
gives B_k<=lambda C_k.

Let rho=log_2(lambda)>1, A(t)=(1+t)^rho-1 for t>0, and
L(u)=log(A(exp u)). Direct differentiation yields

    L''(u)=rho*t*(1+t)^(rho-2)
          *[(1+t)^rho-1-rho*t]/A(t)^2>0, t=exp u.

The bracket is positive because it vanishes at0 and has positive
derivative thereafter. Convexity implies A(t)A(1/t)>=A(1)^2=2.
For x,y>0 this proves
Omega(x,y)<=(x^(1/rho)+y^(1/rho))^rho by substituting
t=(y/x)^(1/rho) into the defining quadratic; the right side is also
at least x+y, since (u+v)^rho-u^rho>=v^rho for u,v>=0, by differentiating
in u. Zero arguments follow by direct evaluation. Induction
gives C_k<=(k+1)^rho, and therefore

    B_n<=lambda^(m+1), n=2^m-1.                       (33)

For an explicit finite margin let delta=1/m<=1/128. Algebra gives
c(c-2)=1+(4+2sqrt(2))delta+(3+2sqrt(2))delta^2<1+7delta+6delta^2.
Thus alpha>(1-delta)/(1+7delta+6delta^2)>15/16; the last claim reduces
to 1>121delta+90delta^2, whose numerator at delta=1/128 is
15578<16384. The first four binomial terms give
(1+1/m)^m>=8/3-1/m+1/(3m^2)>1021/384. Since M>=alpha c^m,

    B_n/M<14848/15315.                                (34)

For M>=64, (19) implies
M P<=M/(2M-1)*(1+9/[16(2M-1)])
< (64/127)(129/128)=129/254<25/49. Combining with (34) proves

    B_n P <371200/750435<1/2                           (35)

for EVERY initial vector in (18) and every m>=128. Uniformly over that
tube, alpha->1 and M/c^m->1, while P T->1 by (19), so

    limsup_(m->infinity) sup_(z in tube) B_n P
          <=lambda/[2 exp(1)]<15/32<1/2.              (36)

The last rational bound follows from lambda<5/2 and exp(1)>8/3. No exact
limiting constant for B_n or benchmark optimization replay was assumed.

## 10. What remains blocked and which shortcuts failed

For the full law from Section1 the exact unresolved object is

    sup { [G_n+2t_n]/sum_l w_0,l(1+t_0d_l)^2/d_l :
           w_0,l>=0, sum w_0,l<=1, t_0>=0,
           t_0(1-sum w_0,l)=0, (t_0,w_0)!=(0,0),
           (t_i,w_i) follow EVERY step of (8)--(9) }.   (37)

No upper bound on (37) at the original benchmark scale and no state
attaining that scale have been established. The exact normal-work identity
(11) still contains signed overstep terms. A new inequality paying them
from the initial radius is a BLOCKED global interface, not a routine step.

Two concrete direct routes were examined. Scalar closure by multiplier
and radius fails by (12)--(16), even before considering long bridges.
The full-axis local expansion is sharper but only local: it includes
the positive feedback term in (22), excludes the growing fast range
(24) and the slow neighbor, and leaves other coefficients unscreened.
Even screening every V_l would not pay finite displacements or core
returns. No exact common-polynomial orbit or independent quadratic
replacement was used or justified.

The tube in (18) has a COMPLETE scoped obstruction (35), including its
true radius and all queries; it cannot be advertised as a solution or
as an obstruction to all initial vectors. Its map to the old scalar
baseline is quantitative, via (20)--(21), not an equality of functions.
The full geometric law is not reduced to an old excluded class.

An exact recall check located6e098f91c4a13444 in the shared proof_attempt
store, after it was absent from the conclusion store. It concerns the
DIFFERENT recurrence H_(j+1)=(H_j,a(13/5)^j,H_j), with a=(13/5)^(-17)
and a free final unit step. Its full-complex obstruction cannot be
transferred to the present b_j=1+a_m c_m^j word merely because both
documents use the letter H. It was not used to strengthen (32).

Searches for a radius/multiplier Lyapunov law returned unrelated
fixed-step proximal and reflection results, not a theorem applicable
to these original long steps. The exact Eberly projection note already
downloaded in the prior round was reread through its publisher PDF text:
David Eberly, "Distance from a Point to an Ellipse, an Ellipsoid, or a
Hyperellipsoid", revised September11,2020,
https://www.geometrictools.com/Documentation/DistancePointEllipseEllipsoid.pdf.
Its surface formulation permits negative interior multipliers; ours
does not. It supplies background only. No external result is a proof
premise, and no unsupported citation was promoted into the fact graph.

The original all-n/all-nonnegative-word goal remains OPEN. The allotted
round ends after sharing and checking this evidence. There is no new
fact, seed, formal gate, publication, finalization, shutdown, or renewal.

# Joint covariance on the original ruler word: exact identity and local obstruction

Status: UNVERIFIED, single joint research draft, max2 round34. No formal
submission, numerical evaluation, solver, machine algebra, replay, consult,
installation, or subagent was used. The assignment is governed by
a23831f2a36b3c4a, with the unchanged max2 allocation confirmed by
48a4276698805f28. This round does not renew itself.

Verdict: the proposed full-covariance mechanism is BLOCKED as a proof of the
whole actual residual kernel. The covariance identity below is exact, but
does not prove its signed domination. There is new uniform negative evidence
against two precise closure rules: declaring every complete child block
nonnegative, and repairing all such blocks with a uniformly bounded number
of linear boundary features. Neither is a counterexample to the complete
original-word kernel. The formal negative directions are explicitly illegal
for the original interpolation array.

The complete block covariance, constant column, signed killing terms, and
final deleted-step correction are retained. The full exact modal state has
m+1 coordinates; the initial position adds one coordinate. A quantitative
boundary payment is proved in Section7. Its availability from the rest of
the original trajectory is NOT proved.

Only accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 is used, for the precise interpolation
necessity/sufficiency and normalization stated below. All other mathematical
ingredients needed here are derived in this one joint draft. In particular
7bc886cf037bdcd4, ff8f8f21b222a81b, and the later load draft
f3d7ccaf615115b7 are research history, not correctness premises.

## 1. Physical word, full shared state, and elementary bounds

Fix an integer m>=128 and set

 epsilon=1/m, lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+epsilon),
 a=(1-epsilon)/c, d_j=1+a*c^j,
 H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j).

Let q=2^m-2. The actual queried word h=(h_0,...,h_(q-1)) is H_m with
its LAST d_0 deleted. The separate final unqueried step is b in [0,2].
Write s_i=sum_(r<i)h_r and S=s_q. An embedded H_K always uses these
same parameters epsilon,c,a, rather than replacing epsilon by1/K.

For arbitrary vectors v,w_0,...,w_(m-1) in ONE real Euclidean space,

 p_ij=product_(r<i)(1-h_r/d_j),
 g_i=v+sum_(j<m)p_ij*w_j,
 x_i=x_0-sum_(r<i)h_r*g_r.

There is no diagonal or independence assumption on their common Gram.
For actual data one common value array f_i and the zero optimum sample
are required; f is normalized to have smoothness1 and minimum0.
In the convention of accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290,

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.       (1)

Every ordered original pair remains a legality requirement. The auxiliary
probability space introduced later changes no function values or geometry.

The first d_j occurs at step2^j-1. Every earlier step is less than d_j,
so p_ij>0 until that step, and p_ij=0 from query2^j onwards. Consequently
g_q=v, and telescoping p_ij-p_(i+1),j=(h_i/d_j)p_ij gives

 sum_(i<q)h_i*p_ij=d_j,
 x_q=x_0-S*v-sum_(j<m)d_j*w_j.                       (2)

We will need only the following elementary parameter bounds. Since
12/5<lambda<29/12,

 12/5<c<39/16,  a>127/312>2/5.

Also c(c-2)>1, hence 0<A_mass:=a/(c-2)<1. Solving the word-sum
recursion S_K:=sum H_K, S_(K+1)=2S_K+d_K, gives

 S_K+1=A_mass*c^K+(1-A_mass)*2^K.

For K>=1 this implies

 S_K<c^K,  S_K/d_K<1/a<5/2.                          (3)

The level ratio satisfies

 d_(k+1)>2d_k for k>=2.                              (4)

Indeed d_(k+1)-2d_k=(c-2)a*c^k-1. At k=2 the quantity before
subtracting1 is (1-epsilon)c(c-2). Since
c(c-2)=1+2lambda(lambda-1)epsilon+lambda^2*epsilon^2
>1+6epsilon, its product with1-epsilon exceeds1+5epsilon-6epsilon^2>1.
It increases with k, proving (4).

The first four levels, retaining the same enclosing epsilon, obey

 d_0=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]<sqrt(2),
 d_1=2-epsilon<2,
 d_2=2+sqrt(2)-lambda*epsilon^2<2+sqrt(2),
 d_3=1+lambda^2*(1+epsilon-epsilon^2-epsilon^3)>4+2sqrt(2).
                                                               (5)

The last inequality uses1-epsilon-epsilon^2>0. These are strict for
every finite m>=128.

## 2. Original-Q identity and the actual unmatched sufficient claim

For ANY nonempty word W=(h_0,...,h_(N-1)) with local clocks s_i and total S_W,
define, on its complete temporal vector sequence,

 J_W(g)=sum_(i<N)[
   2s_(i+1)h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>
   +s_(i+1)||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2].      (6)

For the actual queried word, direct expansion and summation by parts give

 ||x_0||^2-2S*f_q+2sum_(i<q)h_i Q_(i,star)
       +2sum_(i<q)s_(i+1)Q_(i+1,i)=||x_q||^2+J_h(g).  (7)

Here is the full cancellation. GD gives

 2sum h_i Q_(i,star)
 =2sum h_i f_i-||x_0||^2+||x_q||^2
                          +sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2.

Also Q_(i+1,i)=f_(i+1)-f_i+h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>
+||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2/2, and
sum s_(i+1)(f_(i+1)-f_i)=S f_q-sum h_i f_i.
Adding proves (7). All displayed multipliers are nonnegative.

Thus the specific sufficient claim is

 J_h(v,w_0,...,w_(m-1))>=0 for ALL common real vectors, ALL m>=128.
                                                               (8)

If (8) were proved, (7) and the original Q inequalities would imply
2f_q<=||x_0||^2/S. Smooth descent at the last step gives
2f(x_q-bv)<=2f_q-b(2-b)||v||^2<=2f_q. Hence, after true-distance
normalization ||x_0||=1, the entire stated temporal quotient would obey
2f(x_q-bv)<=1/S. This is a conditional quotient bound only. Arbitrary
nonnegative schedules and arbitrary temporal gradients are outside it.

The position square in (7) is exactly (2). Since x_0 is a free formal
vector, including that square does not conceal an extra positivity
assumption: the full quadratic in (x_0,v,w) is PSD iff (8) holds.

## 3. Full conditional-covariance identity, with all coefficients

For a word W as in (6), put Delta_i=g_i-g_(i+1) and

 beta_(-1)=0,
 beta_i=s_(i+1)*(s_i+1), 0<=i<N,
 beta_N=S_W*(S_W+1),
 nu_i=beta_i-beta_(i-1), 0<=i<=N,
 c_i=(h_i-1)*s_(i+1), 0<=i<N.

For the positive ruler steps all nu_i are positive and c_i>=0. More
explicitly nu_0=h_0, nu_N=S_W*h_(N-1), and at an interior query
nu_i=s_i*(h_(i-1)+h_i)+h_i. Expanding adjacent squares proves

 J_W=sum_(i=0)^N nu_i||g_i||^2-sum_(i<N)c_i||Delta_i||^2.
                                                               (9)

For example its diagonal at an interior query is

 kappa_i=2s_i+2h_i-h_i^2,

and its off-diagonal between i and i+1 is
s_(i+1)(h_i-1). The initial diagonal is h_0(2-h_0), and the final
diagonal is S_W. These determine every entry of the temporal tridiagonal
matrix in (6), so (9) also verifies the endpoints.

Let the modal index set be A={infinity,0,...,m-1}, and set
z_infinity=0, z_j=1/d_j. For a prefix polynomial
P_i(z)=product_(r<i)(1-h_r*z), define the row

 r_i=(P_i(z_alpha))_(alpha in A).

Thus r_i,infinity=1, and g_i=sum_alpha r_i,alpha*Z_alpha with
Z_infinity=v and Z_j=w_j. For any nonempty set I of query indices put

 omega_I=sum_(i in I)nu_i,
 ell_I=omega_I^(-1)sum_(i in I)nu_i*r_i,
 Sigma_I=omega_I^(-1)sum_(i in I)nu_i
                   (r_i-ell_I)^T(r_i-ell_I).

These are the complete mean row and covariance matrix of r_i under the
probabilities nu_i/omega_I, not a covariance of independent modal states.
For a disjoint partition I=L union R define
sigma_I=omega_L*omega_R/omega_I. Direct expansion gives

 omega_I*Sigma_I=omega_L*Sigma_L+omega_R*Sigma_R
                +sigma_I*(ell_L-ell_R)^T(ell_L-ell_R). (10)

Indeed each centered row in L is (r_i-ell_L)+(ell_L-ell_I), and the
first cross term sums to zero; the analogous R calculation leaves
omega_L*(ell_L-ell_I)^T(ell_L-ell_I)
+omega_R*(ell_R-ell_I)^T(ell_R-ell_I).
Using ell_I=(omega_L*ell_L+omega_R*ell_R)/omega_I gives the
last matrix in (10), including every off-diagonal entry.

Choose any binary partition tree on {0,...,N}, down to singletons. It
can be aligned with chronological ruler cuts; shared physical endpoints
are assigned to just one of the two query sets. Iterating (10), with
omega_all=beta_N, proves the EXACT identity

 J_W=
  beta_N||sum_alpha ell_all,alpha*Z_alpha||^2
  +sum_(internal I) sigma_I
        ||sum_alpha(ell_L-ell_R)_alpha*Z_alpha||^2
  -sum_(i<N)c_i||sum_alpha h_i*z_alpha*P_i(z_alpha)*Z_alpha||^2.
                                                               (11)

No covariance term has been thrown away. Every coefficient is a finite
sum/product specified above. All the negative killing-step contributions
remain in the last sum. The constant column has coefficient1 in every
ell_I, zero in every ell_L-ell_R, and zero in each jump row. Its full
diagonal is therefore exactly beta_N, and its cross column is retained
in the first square.

Minimizing over the free constant vector v removes only the first square.
Equivalently, (8) asks for the SPECIFIC residual matrix inequality

 sum_(internal I) sigma_I
       (ell_L-ell_R)_res^T(ell_L-ell_R)_res
   >= sum_(i<q)c_i a_i^T a_i,
 a_i=(h_i*p_ij/d_j)_(j<m),                            (12)

on the ACTUAL complete queried word. The left side is beta_q times its
full residual conditional covariance. The right side is its full signed
cost. Equation (12) is an exact restatement, NOT a newly proved bound.
The suffix res denotes restriction to the residual columns0,...,m-1.
In particular positivity of each covariance in (10) does not imply (12).

## 4. Finite full-state transfer and the final boundary

There is no need to enumerate the exponentially many leaves to specify
the complete covariance matrix. For a word W let D_W be the diagonal
matrix with entries P_W(z_alpha), and let K_W,T_W be the symmetric
matrices for the two bilinear kernels

 K_W(z,w)=sum_(i<N)P_i(z)P_i(w)
                     [h_i-h_i^2*(z+w-z*w)/2],

 T_W(z,w)=sum_(i<N)P_i(z)P_i(w)
        [2s_(i+1)h_i-s_(i+1)h_i^2*(z+w-z*w)+h_i*(1-h_i)].

The matrix T_W evaluated at all z_alpha is exactly J_W. This follows
by substituting P_(i+1)(z)=(1-h_i*z)P_i(z) in (6), including its mixed
terms. For a single step d,

 K_[d](z,w)=d-d^2*(z+w-z*w)/2,
 T_[d](z,w)=d(d+1)-d^3*(z+w-z*w).                    (13)

If W=UV, splitting the finite sums at the block boundary gives

 K_UV=K_U+D_U*K_V*D_U,
 T_UV=T_U+D_U*(T_V+2S_U*K_V)*D_U.                    (14)

The offset2S_U*K_V is indispensable: each second-block clock is its
local clock plus S_U. Thus the whole signed child covariance, including
its coupling to every surviving mode and the constant, is transferred.

For H_(k+1)=(H_k,d_k,H_k), abbreviate D=D_Hk, K=K_Hk,
T=T_Hk, s=S_k, and F=diag(1-d_k*z_alpha). Then

 D_H(k+1)=D*F*D,
 K_H(k+1)=K+D*K_[d_k]*D+D*F*K*F*D,
 T_H(k+1)=T+D*(T_[d_k]+2s*K_[d_k])*D
             +D*F*(T+2(s+d_k)*K)*F*D.               (15)

This is an exact finite block identity. It is not claimed that the three
summands are individually PSD. Every matrix entry, including the signed
central contribution, is specified by (13)-(15).

At the last d_0 of H_m, all residual prefix columns have already vanished.
Consequently deleting that step changes ONLY the constant/constant entry:

 K_h=K_Hm-d_0*e_infinity*e_infinity^T,
 T_h=T_Hm-d_0*(2S+d_0+1)*e_infinity*e_infinity^T.      (16)

Here e_infinity is the coordinate unit column. In particular the actual
constant diagonal is S(S+1), not (S+d_0)(S+d_0+1).

The maintained vector-state dimension in (13)-(16) is m+1, and the
augmented original-space dimension including x_0 is m+2. Each symmetric
kernel has (m+1)(m+2)/2 scalar entries. These are finite identities, not
an executed computation or a new computational allowance.
The recursion starts with S_H0=0, D_H0=I, and K_H0=T_H0=0; hence it
specifies all entries without an unknown initial matrix.

The m+1 temporal dimension is genuinely lossless. Take rows at queries
2^j-1 for0<=j<m and at q, and order columns by j followed by infinity.
The residual diagonal at rowj is p_(2^j-1),j>0, its earlier columns are
zero, and the final row is (0,...,0,1). This square evaluation matrix
is triangular with nonzero diagonal. Therefore an exact linear
representation of ALL gradient traces cannot use fewer than m+1
coordinates. This is not a lower bound for every conceivable nonlinear
proof or probability representation.

## 5. Complete negative child blocks on the ACTUAL chronology

The proposed closure rule tested here is precise:

 "For every initial ruler block H_K, its entire signed energy J_HK is
  nonnegative on all inherited surviving reciprocal-mode states, perhaps
  after conditioning on its endpoint gradients and displacement."

This assertion is FALSE. The construction below covers every query of
the complete block. It does not select only a negative individual step.

Fix K>=4 with K+2^K<=m. Put N=2^K-1 and R=N+1=2^K. Use only the R
residual modes j=K,...,K+R-1, so all selected modes survive H_K. Set
v=0 and all other modal coefficients zero. On this ACTUAL prefix the
steps are h_i=d_(nu_2(i+1)), where nu_2 is the exponent of2 in i+1.

Define z_r=1/d_(K+r), 0<=r<R, and the square matrix

 V_(i,r)=P_i(z_r), 0<=i,r<=N.

The polynomials P_i have distinct degrees i and leading coefficients
L_i=(-1)^i product_(t<i)h_t. Hence, by triangular conversion to
monomials and the Vandermonde determinant,

 Ddet:=det V
   =[product_(i=0)^N product_(t<i)h_t]
                        product_(0<=r<s<=N)(z_r-z_s)>0.       (17)

The two signs cancel because there are N(N+1)/2 factors of each sign.
Thus EVERY specified real vector of all block query gradients
A=(A_0,...,A_N) has the following explicit modal coefficients:

 w_(K+r)=Ddet^(-1) sum_(i=0)^N
                (-1)^(i+r) det(V with row i and column r deleted)*A_i.
                                                               (18)

Empty determinants have value1. Every determinant in (17)-(18) denotes
the finite signed permutation sum of its displayed entries; no unknown
equation, root, or numerical inversion is hidden in this specification.
The cofactor identity V*adj(V)=Ddet*I proves g_i=A_i at EVERY query
0<=i<=N. The modal Gram of these scalar coefficients is real PSD
of rank at most1; no negative Gram is used.

For 3<=k<K put i_k=2^k-1 and

 kappa_k=2S_k+2d_k-d_k^2.

Let eta_k be the amplitude of the pulse at query i_k; in the query
vector used in (18), A_(i_k)=eta_k and all other entries vanish.
An isolated pulse has energy exactly kappa_k*eta_k^2. Its preceding
step contributes S_k*eta_k^2, and its d_k killing step contributes
[S_k+2d_k-d_k^2]*eta_k^2. The entire rest of H_K contributes zero.

These complete pulse energies are uniformly negative. At k=3,
S_3=4d_0+2d_1+d_2<6+5sqrt(2), while d_3>4+2sqrt(2) by (5).
The function 2d-d^2 decreases for d>1. Therefore

 kappa_3
 <2(6+5sqrt(2))+2(4+2sqrt(2))-(4+2sqrt(2))^2
 =-(4+2sqrt(2)).                                    (19)

For k>=4, (3)-(5) give d_k>=d_4>2d_3>21/2, whence

 kappa_k<d_k*(7-d_k)<-d_k^2/3.                       (20)

Distinct pulse indices differ by at least8. The temporal matrix in (9)
is tridiagonal, so there are no cross terms between these pulses. On
the entire pulse subspace,

 J_HK=sum_(k=3)^(K-1)kappa_k*eta_k^2<0 for nonzero eta. (21)

This is a (K-3)-dimensional negative subspace of the complete formal
child kernel on the selected high modes. Equations (17)-(18) realize
it without altering a single physical step or its enclosing parameter.

In particular the single fixed block H_4 works for EVERY m>=128:
use modes4,...,19, put A_7=1 and the other15 query values zero. Both
endpoint gradients vanish, and J_H4<-(4+2sqrt(2)).

There is also an explicit zero-displacement counterexample. In H_5 use
modes5,...,36 and prescribe all32 query values by

 g_7=d_4, g_15=-d_3, g_i=0 otherwise.                 (22)

Then g_0=g_31=0 and

 sum_(i<31)h_i*g_i=d_3*d_4-d_4*d_3=0,
 x_31=x_0,
 J_H5=kappa_3*d_4^2+kappa_4*d_3^2
      <-(4+2sqrt(2))*d_4^2-d_4^2*d_3^2/3<0.          (23)

Thus endpoint gradients and positions alone cannot make this entire
signed child nonnegative. The full correlated inherited state matters.

## 6. Unbounded boundary-feature requirement and explicit all-Q failure

On the pulse subspace (21), endpoint gradients already vanish. Its
displacement is -sum_(k=3)^(K-1)d_k*eta_k. Imposing zero displacement
is exactly ONE independent linear relation, leaving dimension K-4
when K>=5. The signed energy is negative definite on that subspace.

Consider ANY proposed extra boundary correction which, on this inherited
modal state, depends on at most r scalar linear features, with any real
symmetric coefficient matrix. Write it as (Lw)^T C (Lw), rank L<=r.
The map L is allowed to depend on the word and all its parameters.
If r<K-4, its kernel intersects the zero-displacement negative subspace
nontrivially. At such a state the correction is exactly zero and J_HK<0.
Hence no such correction can make every inherited child state nonnegative.

This rank argument even allows an arbitrary sign for C. It concerns
linear features and quadratic boundary charges; it does not refute
nonlinear conditioning, arbitrary-dimensional covariance, interblock
couplings, or other uses of original Q inequalities.

For every fixed r, choose K>=r+5 and m>=max(128,K+2^K). Thus a
uniformly bounded feature dimension cannot close all these children.
Choosing the largest K with K+2^K<=m gives a necessary feature count
at least K-4, which grows with m. It is not asserted to be a sharp
minimal dimension for the full original-kernel proof.

The negative directions are NOT legal smooth-convex transcripts. In
any nonzero pulse combination let i be the first nonzero-gradient query.
All earlier gradients, including g_(i-1), are zero, so GD has
x_i=x_(i-1)=x_0. For EVERY choice of shared values,

 Q_(i,i-1)+Q_(i-1,i)=||g_i-g_(i-1)||^2>0.             (24)

The necessary all-pair inequalities (1) therefore fail. In (22) the
specific pair is (6,7), and its sum equals d_4^2. Initial position may
be chosen as1, but that does not repair (24) or turn it into a normalized
legal hard function. For the homogeneous feature-rank argument it may
instead be set to0; scaling the pulse also defeats any fixed endpoint
charge at a nonzero fixed initial position.

These examples also do NOT show T_h is indefinite. After H_K the high
modal state specified by (18) is nonzero and continues along the actual
remaining physical word. Its later energy has not been bounded from
above and may pay the early deficit. The terminal norm of the complete
word is not replaced by that of H_K.

## 7. An explicit paid boundary bound for the growing state

This subsection gives a concrete sufficient local payment, rather than
merely saying to retain more coordinates. It does NOT prove that the
original suffix supplies the payment.

Fix 3<=K<m and use arbitrary common vectors in the modes j>=K, with
all lower modes zero and an arbitrary constant v. Put N=2^K-1,
r=m-K, and let the surviving modal vectors at the end of H_K be

 b_j=P_N(1/d_j)*w_j, K<=j<m.

Every step in H_K is at most d_(K-1)<d_K/2 by (4). For0<=x<=1/2,
log(1-x)>=-2x, obtained by integrating1/(1-x)<=2 from0 to x. Thus

 P_N(1/d_j)>=exp(-2S_K/d_j)>exp(-5),
 0<P_i(1/d_j)/P_N(1/d_j)<=exp(5).                   (25)

All factors are positive and at most1, and (3) gives the exponent bound.
The exact jump is

 Delta_i=h_i sum_(j>=K) [P_i(1/d_j)/(d_j P_N(1/d_j))]*b_j.

Cauchy--Schwarz in the finite j sum, retaining arbitrary common vector
cross terms, gives

 ||Delta_i||^2 <= r*exp(10)*h_i^2/d_K^2
                                      *sum_(j>=K)||b_j||^2.

Furthermore c_i=(h_i-1)s_(i+1)<=h_i*s_(i+1), and

 sum_i c_i*h_i^2
 <=d_(K-1)^2 sum_i s_(i+1)h_i
 =d_(K-1)^2*(S_K^2+sum_i h_i^2)/2
 <=d_(K-1)^2*S_K^2 <d_K^2*S_K^2/4.

Dropping only the nonnegative first sum in (9) proves the actual bound

 J_HK >= -[r*exp(10)/4]*S_K^2*sum_(j>=K)||b_j||^2.   (26)

The price is explicit, applies to the complete correlated high-mode
state and arbitrary v, and grows linearly with r in this chosen state
norm. It is not asserted to be sharp. For the example (22), ANY valid
boundary payment must in particular exceed the positive magnitude
displayed in (23).

Keeping the full state therefore avoids an algebraic information loss,
but does not provide a free energy budget. In the complete original
trajectory every residual b_j is ultimately killed. One cannot simply
attach (26) to an intermediate boundary and erase its cost at the last
boundary. No telescoping family of available payments has been proved.

## 8. Direct attempts, comparison with the scalar approach, and remaining gap

Attempt A retained all the conditional covariance in (10)-(11) and tried
to certify each whole ruler child as nonnegative before recombination.
This is refuted by (17)-(21), already at the ACTUAL complete H_4 prefix.
Its failure includes the lower-step contributions preceding the killing
step and the full block endpoint; it is stronger than the old observation
that an individual killing summand can be negative.

Attempt B allowed arbitrary quadratic repairs from endpoints and a fixed
number of extra linear boundary features. The complete H_5 example
(22)-(23) defeats endpoints alone, and Section6 defeats every uniform
finite feature count on larger actual prefixes. These are precise
failures of the tested local rules, not a prohibition on probability.

Attempt C kept the full m+1-dimensional transfer and all its covariances.
Equations (13)-(16) are exact and (26) prices a local repair, but the
global signed inequality (12) remains unmatched. This is where the
mechanism is BLOCKED. No available bound shows that the cross-block
variance squares dominate every original killing cost after the actual
constant square is minimized and the final d_0 is deleted.

Unlike the old scalar carry, no child covariance is discarded. Unlike
the earlier birth-coordinate pair/triple-square proposal, this draft
does not assume any b_i/u_i or alternating-load margin. It neither
reproves the earlier positive-entry/stability claims nor resolves those
unproved signs indirectly. The exact covariance organization alone is
not uniform positivity evidence. The new evidence is the uniform
complete-child obstruction, its feature-rank extension, and its paid
boundary estimate, with all-Q legality separately settled.

Even a future proof of (12) would close only the original-Q interface
(7) on the specified m-dependent reciprocal temporal quotient. It would
not establish the arbitrary-n, arbitrary-nonnegative-schedule profile,
and would not make these formal negative pulses actual hard functions.
The normalization remains W=2R; no fixed-word theorem is presented as
the answer to the original Goal.

## 9. Retrieval and scope audit

The shared-memory review found later load progress beyond ff8f8f21b222a81b:
f3d7ccaf615115b7 treats rows2,3 and all rows>=8 in its unverified joint
draft; rows4..7, the signed margins, and final quantitative charges were
not settled there. None is an input to this proof. The earlier
single-initial-mixture obstruction4cd717742b582ea8 is not re-opened.

Matlas search for matrix conditional-covariance/energy identities returned
"TimeoutError: The read operation timed out", with no results. Primary
web fallback led to Lami, Hirche, Adesso, Winter, "Schur complement
inequalities for covariance matrices and monogamy of quantum correlations",
arXiv:1607.05285v2 (2016). The exact PDF and extracted HTML text were saved
under downloads/1607.05285.*; the definitions and Theorem1 proof were read.
That theorem assumes a PSD tripartite covariance matrix and compares its
Schur complements V_ABC/V_BC <= V_AC/V_C. The signed matrix here is not
known PSD; the complete child can be indefinite by Section5. Its proof
therefore cannot supply the missing assumption. No external theorem is
used as a premise, and no external citation is silently promoted to a fact.

No formal verification was permitted in this round, so every new result
above remains an UNVERIFIED joint draft. The original Goal and full actual
chronological kernel stay OPEN. The one allocated round ends with this
scoped mechanism failure and does not authorize another search round.
